# World Zero — Frontend Style Guide

**Design system reference.** This document describes _intent and constraints_ — not implementation. For exact values, see `index.css` (CSS variables) and `factions.ts` (faction config). The code is the source of truth for colors, sizes, and spacing.

---

## 0. Philosophy

World Zero is a whimsical real-world game. The UI should feel like a **handmade artifact** — paper textures, ink, collage, field journals, newspaper clippings — not a SaaS dashboard. Every faction has its own visual language expressed through its card archetype. The overall aesthetic is "eccentric design student meets community ARG": colorful, slightly chaotic, but always readable.

**The one rule:** weird is good. Standard is bad. When in doubt, ask: would an eccentric design student be proud of this or embarrassed by it?

---

## 1. UX Principles

These are non-negotiable and take precedence over any visual specification.

1. **Responsive over pixel-perfect.** Define reasonable defaults (min/max widths, fluid spacing). Only pin exact dimensions when the design genuinely requires it. Cards, panels, and grids should adapt to available space.

2. **Set defaults, override selectively.** Typography, spacing, colors, borders — set them once at the theme level. Override individual elements only when they need to differ. If you find yourself repeating a value across components, it belongs in the CSS variable system.

   **A shared card is resized by its MOUNT, through an inherited custom property — never by a variant prop (#1137).** One component now serves every surface and both form factors (ADR-0067 for the praxis card), which means its geometry is *nine bespoke frames' worth* of inline style and a host page cannot reach any of it. The two obvious escapes are both wrong: a `compact` prop forks all nine internals for a decision that is purely about the container, and a class selector loses to the inline style it is trying to beat. A custom property is the one thing that crosses that boundary — it inherits down into an inline `var()`, so the container answers the question, and a surface that sets nothing renders byte-identically. `frameBase`'s `flex: 1 1 var(--praxis-card-basis, 394px)` against `.praxis-gallery` is the shape: **the default is today's value, and the override is a single declaration on the mount.**

   Two things this only works if you get right. **Pick the value by where the row WRAPS, not by how wide you want the card**: with `flex-grow: 1` the cards then stretch to fill the line, so the basis chooses a card *count* and the count chooses the width. The task detail's cards were rendering at 494–560px — half again the 380–398px the skins were drawn at — precisely because a 394px basis fitted only two across the column and both grew. And **an accompanying `min-width` silently caps the whole mechanism**: `frameBase`'s 280px floor is what holds one card per row on a phone, and it also swallows any basis below it, so the property is only meaningful in [280, 394]. State that range where the variable is declared or the next editor will set 240 and wonder why nothing moved.

3. **Single source of truth for style.** Colors, typography, and spacing live in CSS custom properties (`index.css`). Components reference variables — never hardcode hex values or pixel sizes. Dark mode works automatically through the cascade. **Faction colors live in `index.css` and nowhere else.** `factions.ts` maps a slug to a theme key and hands out `var()` references (`factionCssVar` / `factionFill`); it holds no hex of its own. It used to carry a mirror table that this document asked you to keep in sync by hand. The mirror drifted — UA sat on the *unaffiliated spectrum's* orange for months — and, being a literal, it could never hold a dark value at all, so every JS-sourced accent stayed at its light hue on a dark page. A second copy that must be synced is the failure, not the sync (#1269).

4. **If you can't use it, you can't see it.** Buttons, menu items, and actions that the user lacks permission for (level gate, role, status) should not render at all. Don't show disabled controls — hide them. This is already the pattern in the codebase; maintain it.

   **Validation belongs in business logic, not UX.** Gate rules (level thresholds, faction rules, anti-self checks, one-per-task rules) live in backend services. The backend is authoritative.
   - API responses include explicit `can_X` flags (`can_flag`, `can_sign_up`, `can_create_additional_character`, `allowed_modes`, `eligible_for_current_user`, etc.) computed server-side.
   - The frontend consumes those flags and hides controls accordingly. Do not re-implement the rule in a component.
   - No hardcoded rule thresholds in the frontend. If you're writing `level >= 4` in a component, the backend should be returning a flag instead.
   - Disabled state (`<button disabled>`) is only for in-flight async and form validity — never for rule-based denial.

   **Hiding the control does not hide the SPACE it was given (#1138).** All nine task-detail skins gated their one action cell behind `hasAction` and then pinned the column around it — 420 to 520px, unconditionally — so a logged-out visitor got a faction-framed panel sized for a sign-up button, wrapped around a 168px score box. Nine files, one omission, and the gated half looked correct in every review of it. A layout pinned in the same component that gates its contents is where this hides: the ternary that reads `desktop ? 440 : "100%"` answers *which form factor*, and the question it also has to answer is *whether there is anything to hold*. §1.1 is the general form — pin a dimension only where the design genuinely requires it — and a width that exists to seat an absent control does not require it. Note that this cuts against reaching for a shared `PANEL_WIDTH`: the spread across the skins is dress, so what gets hoisted is the *shape* of the expression (`actionColumnSize`), never the number.

5. **Every button does something.** Don't render an interactive control unless it has a handler that does real work on press. No placeholder buttons, no "coming soon" stubs, no controls that render but no-op. If the feature isn't built yet, the control isn't on the page yet. This is stricter than #4: #4 hides controls the user _can't_ use; this rule says even the _author_ can't leave a dead control behind.

6. **Faction identity cascades from the card archetype.** Anything associated with a faction (profile headers, praxis bylines, proposal wrappers, feed items) should reuse the faction's card aesthetic. Change the card archetype once and every faction-branded element updates. Don't create parallel styling for each context.

7. **The code is the spec.** This document describes design _intent_. When this document and the code disagree, update whichever is wrong. Don't let them drift.

---

## 2. Tech Stack (Frontend)

- **Framework:** React (functional components + hooks only)
- **Styling:** Tailwind utilities + CSS custom properties. Inline styles are acceptable for truly dynamic values (rotations, faction-specific backgrounds) but repeated patterns should be CSS classes.
- **Fonts:** self-hosted woff2 (see Typography section)
- **Icons:** CSS shapes, SVG, or typographic characters — no icon libraries
- **Animations:** CSS transitions only; keep subtle
- **Theme:** Light and dark mode via CSS custom properties on `:root` and `[data-theme="dark"]`

---

## 3. Color System

All color values are CSS custom properties defined in `index.css`. See that file for the complete list.

**Key groups:**

- **Page:** `--color-bg-page`, `--color-bg-surface`, `--color-bg-surface-alt`
- **Text:** `--color-text-primary`, `--color-text-secondary`, `--color-text-tertiary` — three tiers, and the third one is a **temperature** rather than a weight (see below)
- **Borders:** `--color-border`, `--color-border-strong`
- **Factions:** `--faction-{slug}` (primary), `--faction-{slug}-light` (tint), `--faction-{slug}-border`, `--faction-{slug}-on-night` (the primary as ink on a surface that is dark in BOTH themes — `:root`-only, see below)
- **Faction cards:** `--faction-{slug}-card-bg`, `--faction-{slug}-card-text`, `--faction-{slug}-card-accent`, `--faction-{slug}-card-font`, and — since #2361 — the frame: `--faction-{slug}-card-radius`, `--faction-{slug}-card-border`
- **Functional:** `--color-success`, `--color-danger`, `--color-warning`. Danger and warning each carry a `-veil` (a tinted fill behind a banner), an `-edge` (a 1–2px rule) and a `-ring` (the stronger outline an outline-button wears), per theme. Danger additionally carries `--color-on-danger`, the ink for text painted **on** the red. There is no `-on-warning` and no `--color-success-*` ladder. Mint a rung when a surface needs it, not before. (This line used to promise a `-light` and a `-border` for all three; only one ever existed, and #1169 deleted it as dead.) **The `-on-warning` half of that is now overdue rather than deliberate (#1413):** two buttons *do* fill with `--color-warning` — the praxis detail's fail-confirm and the admin queue's — and both wear `btn-primary`'s inherited `--color-bg-page`, which is a statement about the page and not about legibility on amber. It clears, at **4.57:1** light and 11.14 dark, so this is hygiene rather than a defect; the reason it is not fixed here is that fixing one of the two sites is exactly the one-at-a-time failure #1168 names, and the other lives on a page outside that issue's scope.
- **Votes:** there is no global vote ladder. Every vote control is a faction skin painting from its own `--faction-{key}-vote-*` family, and `na` reads `--spectrum-glow-1…5`. A `--vote-1`…`-5` ramp was declared here for a long time with no reader in either cascade and no dark half at all; #1661 deleted it — the same shape as the `-light`/`-border` promise #1169 removed one line above, and the same lesson: **a documented token with no consumer teaches the wrong palette to whoever reads this file next.**
- **The rainbow:** `--faction-default-stop-1` through `-stop-7` (red → orange → yellow → green → teal → blue → magenta) as scalars, plus the nine gradient cuts composed from them. This is the site's only rainbow — see below. **The cuts are declared once, and are NOT restated in the dark cascade (#1661).** `data-theme` is set on `document.documentElement`, so `:root` and `[data-theme="dark"]` match the same element and a cut's `var(--faction-default-stop-N)` resolves against whichever stop declaration wins there. Re-cutting the spectrum for dark is editing the seven stops and nothing else — which is what the light block had always promised, restated in ~1 KB of duplicate source that could only ever drift. Their declaring selector is `:root, [data-theme]` rather than bare `:root`, for the reason immediately below; the value is still stated once.

**Dark mode** is handled by `[data-theme="dark"]` overrides in `index.css`. Components should use `var(--faction-everymen-card-bg)` — never `dark ? '#1e1a10' : '#fffef5'`.

**Rule:** If you're about to hardcode a hex value in a component, stop. Add it as a CSS variable first.

### An alias is declared on `:root, [data-theme]`, and a literal is not (#1839)

The bullet above says a spectrum cut needs no dark twin because `:root` and `[data-theme="dark"]` match the same element. That is true of `documentElement` and of nothing else, and the app has somewhere else: `pages/characterProfile/archetypes/profileSkin.tsx` renders `data-theme` on the skin container, which the Singularity and S.N.I.D.E. profile bodies set to `dark` so a faction pinned to one theme never mutates the global one.

**A `var()` substitutes against the element it is declared on, so a subtree only re-substitutes if something re-declares it there.** Under the *global light* theme those two profiles rendered half flipped. `[data-theme="dark"]` in `index.css` is a bare attribute selector, so it matches the nested container and every **directly declared** token went dark inside it; `:root` matches `documentElement` alone, so every **alias** kept the light value it had already resolved to on the root and inherited that into the subtree. Signup carries no faction gate, so any faction's praxis card can land in there — which is where it showed worst.

Three things worth carrying.

**The failure sorted cleanly by shape, and the shape named the fix.** Every broken token was an alias and every intact one was a literal, which says the bug is the *declaring selector* rather than any value — §"the distribution is the diagnosis" (#1792) arriving at a scope instead of a cascade. So the aliases are declared on `:root, [data-theme]`: one value, one place, and any element carrying a theme re-declares it and re-substitutes against its own referents. Specificity is untouched — `[data-theme]` and `[data-theme="dark"]` are both (0,1,0) and the dark block sits later in the file, so it still wins wherever it applies. **This covers every wrapper anyone nests later, not just today's two.**

**Restating the thirty in the dark cascade is the rejected fix, and it is rejected because it works.** It is precisely the ~1 KB of duplication #1661 deleted, reintroduced one issue at a time; #1827 shipped exactly one instance of it (`--faction-wow-stamp-bg`) with a comment warning against generalising, and #1839 deleted that restatement rather than growing it. So did changing the two theme-pinned skins to declare their own values: that treats a general scoping bug as two components' problem and leaves the mechanism broken for the next skin that nests a theme. **A fix that scales with the number of symptoms is a fix at the wrong tier.**

**Only aliases belong in that scope.** A literal needs nothing — the dark block already re-declares the ones that flip and the rest inherit unchanged — so putting one there is noise pretending to be a rule. The thirty sit in twelve blocks split *in place* rather than gathered into one, because several of the notes around them are load-bearing about their neighbours: the UA praxis-card exception states in writing that its whole `--faction-ua-card-*` namespace is named in one place, and lifting four of its ten declarations out would have falsified that. `frozenThemeAliases.test.ts` holds both halves — the inventory of the shape, and the assertion that each member is declared by a selector a nested `[data-theme]` matches. **A ratio cannot see this class of bug and neither can a value assertion; the guard has to read the selector.**

### The functional families have a className half, and the tier split is the point (#1609)

`.danger-text`, `.danger-edge` and `.warning-text` (`@layer components`) are how a `className` reaches `--color-danger` / `--color-danger-edge` / `--color-warning`. They exist because a **stock Tailwind ramp is outside the cascade**: forty-one sites across twenty-three pages painted their error chrome `text-red-600 border-red-300`, which reads Tailwind's own hexes and cannot flip, so every error banner in the app rendered its light-theme red on the dark page. That class is now zero, and `local/no-raw-colour-values` (#1853) is what keeps it there.

Four things worth carrying.

**A repaint that only breaks in one theme leaves no diff in the other, which is exactly why it survived.** `text-red-600` **is** `#dc2626` — `--color-danger`'s light value to the byte. Light was pixel-correct the whole time and every screenshot, every review and every axe run agreed; only dark was ever wrong, and dark rendered *a* red, just not this theme's. When a token and a stock utility share a hex, the utility is not a shortcut to the token, it is a copy of one of its two values. (The one place the light render *does* move is the rule: `border-red-300` is `#fca5a5`, and `--color-danger-edge` at 30 % over the page composites nearer `#efb6b2`. It is a drawn mark carrying no words, so it is a taste call, not a contrast one.)

**One class per TOKEN, not one class per banner.** Eighteen of the twenty-three sites repeat `font-body content-text … border-2 px-3 py-2` near-verbatim and genuinely want a shared `<ErrorBanner>`. Folding the type and the padding into a stylesheet class would have looked like the same cleanup and would have frozen one archetype's metrics one tier below where they belong — §1.2's rule reversed. These three carry paint and nothing else, which is what the drift actually was; the component, when it comes, reads them. `.danger-edge` sets a border-*colour* only, for the mechanical half of the same reason: `border-2` lives in Tailwind's utilities layer and beats any shorthand declared in `@layer components`.

**Approaching a limit is not an error.** The eight length counters (Contact, Edit Character, Propose Task) all turned red at 90 %, and the propose-task fields then showed a *second*, genuinely-at-the-limit message in the same red — two states, one colour, and the louder one first. The approach state is `--color-warning` and only the over-limit copy stays danger. That also buys contrast rather than spending it: measured with `utils/contrast.ts`, `--color-warning` reads **4.57:1** light / **11.14:1** dark on the page and 4.89 / 10.18 on `--color-bg-surface` composited over it, against `--color-danger`'s 4.40:1 light — the global-ink debt recorded at `--color-danger-veil`, which the counters now step off rather than onto.

**`text-muted` was suspected of being a bypass and is not.** The counters' quiet arm resolves through `tailwind.config.ts`, where `muted: "var(--color-text-secondary)"` — a var()-backed row, not a stock ramp. Grepping `index.css` for the class name finds nothing and reads as a bypass; the repo's own colour utilities are declared in the Tailwind config by design, precisely so `index.css` stays the only home for a colour *value*. **Check both files before calling a utility untokenised.**

### The third text tier is a TEMPERATURE, not a weight (#1549)

The neutral vocabulary is three tiers, and only the first step is a step in contrast. `--color-text-primary` → `--color-text-secondary` is a 3.0× drop in ratio; `--color-text-secondary` → `--color-text-tertiary` is a **hue swing at held weight** — a lavender against warm greys, in both cascades, at essentially the same luminance.

**That is arithmetic, not taste.** Secondary bottomed out *under* AA on the selected filter row — 4.05:1 light, 3.80:1 dark — so a third rung genuinely *quieter* than secondary and still clearing AA had nowhere at all to stand. (#1549 wrote that band as [4.50, 4.96] measured on `--switch-well`; #1715 found the row one wash deeper and the band was already empty.) There is no room below secondary for a step anyone could see. A palette can afford two weight tiers over an AA floor and this one already spends both, so the third tier had to find a different axis or stop claiming to exist.

It had been claiming to exist without one. Light ran `#6b6050` / `#6c6358` — one hex step per channel, **ΔE 3.4**, 5.60:1 against 5.37:1 on the page — so every surface reaching for tertiary to sound quieter than secondary got nothing back, and had every reason to hardcode a grey instead. Dark had already made the right call (a lavender at h 296°, ΔE 38.3) and was left untouched; light was walked to the same hue at h 298°, C 20, weight held to within 0.2 L\*. **The dark cascade was the reference and not the patient** — repointing it would have moved ~460 `.eyebrow` sites for no defect.

Three things worth carrying.

**Cool-on-warm recedes at equal luminance**, so "quieter" survives as a perception after it stops being a ratio. A desaturated cool ink on warm paper reads as further away; that is what buys the hierarchy the contrast ladder could not. Chroma is dialled *down* from dark's 27 to 20 for the same reason in reverse — a mid-tone at C 27 reads as a colour on cream and as a tint on near-black, so matching the number would not have matched the voice.

**Neither cascade makes tertiary dimmer than secondary, and dark makes it louder** (8.94:1 against 8.33:1 on the page since #1715). If you find a comment claiming tertiary is "one step down", it predates this and is wrong; the file had two, and both were corrected rather than acted on. A rule that routes *away* from tertiary because it is "too faint" is the tell — measure before you believe it, since the same rule in `.filter-factions` cost contrast in dark while claiming to buy it in light.

**A contrast manifest cannot see this class of bug**, which is why the fix ships with a non-ratio guard beside the ratios. Both inks were comfortably AA-clear on every ground the whole time: the maths knows what an ink sits **on** and never what it sits **beside**. `deltaE76` and a floor of 20 across the three tiers in both cascades is #1449's deferred upgrade — that block guards the card family's alarm/notice split with a hex *inequality* and says in its own ponytail that a distance floor is the real answer, to be minted when a repaint walks two inks apart. This was that repaint. **Two tiers a viewer cannot tell apart are one tier with two names**, and a hex inequality passes them both.

### AA is a floor, and the two quiet inks were sitting on it (#1715)

The tiers above were legible everywhere and *comfortable* nowhere. Dark secondary read 4.87:1 on the surface the profile actually draws on — clearing the floor by 0.37 while carrying `23 / 60 pts this level` — and both inks were **under** AA on a selected filter row. Both cascades are now lifted to **AAA 7:1** on the app's own two stocks: light `#6b6050` / `#656081` → `#554b3c` / `#4d4868`, dark `#988c78` / `#9490c0` → `#b9ac97` / `#b3b0d6`. Hue and role are held; the axis that moved is L*. The most-painted ink in the repo is not where a floor belongs.

Three things this leaves behind.

**Composite the ground, or you will measure the wrong surface and clear it.** The first measurement of this bug used `--color-bg-page` and read fine. The panels are `--color-bg-surface-alt`, which is *alpha in dark* — `rgba(255,255,255,0.06)` over `#13121a` composites to `#212028`, not `#13121a` — and that ground is the tighter one in **both** cascades, for opposite reasons: a darker sheet than the page in light, a lighter one in dark. One layer further down is the selected filter row, page → `--switch-well` → `--switch-thumb`, two washes of `--color-text-primary`, and it is the tightest neutral stock in the app. Two independent hand measurements during grilling each missed by exactly one compositing layer, which is why `utils/contrast.ts` exists and why every number in this section came out of it — the manifest's `veil` now takes an array for that reason.

**A hue-family tier has a ceiling, and it is the ΔE floor above rather than the palette.** Dark `--color-text-primary` is a warm cream and dark secondary is the same family, so the pair is separated by lightness alone: the lift spends 12 of the 32.7 ΔE between them and lands at **20.7** against a floor of 20. Tertiary escapes by hue and has 36.9 to spend. There is about 1 L* of headroom left on dark secondary — a further lift has to move primary, which is a different blast radius. **The rung that shares a hue with its neighbour is the one that runs out first.**

**A global lift moves both ways, and the always-dark sheets pay.** Lifting light means *darkening*, so every light paper sheet gained about two ratio points (UA 4.89 → 7.09, everymen 4.56 → 6.61) while the three sheets that are near-black in **both** themes lost about one (S.N.I.D.E. 3.18 → 2.19, Singularity 3.29 → 2.27, the Ephemerists plate 2.92 → 2.01). Those three were already unreachable — no light-cascade neutral clears a near-black sheet — and the answer is the per-faction `--label-ink` the seam exists for, not a softer global. Two of the three cover themselves already (the S.N.I.D.E. voice moved its shared slots onto flipping paper, both Singularity chassis repoint the globals), which left the Ephemerists plate as the one surface to fix, and **#1754 is that report accepted rather than a patch around the global.**

**A report accepted is not a regression reverted, and the distinction decides what you change (#1754).** 2.92:1 was already far under AA; the lift deepened a failing surface instead of breaking a passing one, so nothing about the global moved. `.eph-plate-sheet` — the column both Ephemerists detail pages wear — now sets `--label-ink: var(--faction-ephemerists-plate-quiet)`, the first faction repoint of that seam on a praxis wall. Two things worth carrying.

**Which of a faction's quiet inks you take is a GROUND question, and the seam's declared default can be the wrong one.** That declaration names `--faction-{key}-card-muted`, and for seven keys that is right. Ephemerists' card sheet is the cornice band, so its `-card-muted` is `-plate-band-quiet`, measured on `#0a0c15`; this column's grounds are the page, the plate and the panel cell, which are what `-plate-quiet` was minted and measured for (§ "Contrast is a pairing", #1173). Both clear — the wrong one clears *higher* — and taking the higher number would still have been the #1028 error of moving an ink off the ground it was measured on, and would have put a fourth warm tone on one sheet. **Read the default as "the faction's quiet ink for THIS ground", not as a token name.**

**A theme-invariant register buys one ink with both cascades' margin.** The plate declares nothing under `[data-theme="dark"]` (#1627), so `-plate-quiet` reads the same 6.38 / 5.98 / 5.52 in either theme — which means dark gives up about 2.3 points (8.33 → 5.98 on the plate) to close a 2.01 in light. That trade is correct here and is *not* the trade #1715 refused: the neutral it argued to AAA is the most-painted ink in the repo, and this is one faction's ink on one sheet, landing well over the 4.5 floor.

**And repointing the seam repaints faction-dressed chrome, which is the point, while ADR-0061's neutral chrome is held by `.card-on-page` (#1606).** What actually moved on the plate is the comment leaves' owner controls and the thread heading — bare `.label-caption` / `.label-heading` on `-plate-bg`, i.e. exactly the labels sitting at 2.01:1. The steward bar and the report card do not move, because `.card-on-page` pins the seam back to the global. **Before repointing a seam, list what reads it bare under that root; the ones with an inline colour are not the ones you are fixing.**

### A surface that does not flip needs an ink that does not flip (#1792)

The register above is a *trade* — one ink, both cascades' margin — because the plate is a light sheet in light and a dark sheet in dark. **A surface that is the same in both themes is not a trade at all, and getting it wrong is invisible in review.** `--sky-bg` is declared in `:root` with no dark override; the Constellation paints its own night canvas, deliberately, so a light-theme reader meets a dark stage on a light page. The score numeral under each orb was inked with `factionCssVar(slug)` — a token that *does* flip — so in light theme that permanently-dark stage was painted with hues tuned for a light page ground. Four of seven fell under AA: ephemerists 3.22, everymen 3.29, singularity 3.71, ua 3.92, against a dark half where all seven cleared 5.51 or better.

Three things worth carrying, and the first is the one that reprices this class of bug.

**The distribution is the diagnosis.** The nightly reported four faction hues failing at one selector, which reads as four hues being too dark and prices as "invent four new values". It is not. *Every* failure was a light-theme value and *every* dark value cleared, which says the bug is the flip and not the hue — one bug wearing four hats. When a report's failures sort cleanly by cascade, the cascade is the mechanism, and a per-symptom fix is four bugs' worth of new colour for one bug's worth of cause.

**The ink that clears the ground already existed, locked inside `[data-theme="dark"]`.** So minting the tier #1766 declined to mint — "a per-faction hue guaranteed to clear AA on this ground" — meant designing nothing: `--faction-{slug}-on-night` holds each faction's dark hex, already drawn, already reviewed. **Before pricing a new colour, check whether the other cascade has already shipped it.** It must be a *literal*, not `var(--faction-{slug})`, because a `var()` resolves where it is read and would flip exactly like the hue it aliases — which is the one case the "no mirror table" rule (#1269) cannot delete its way out of, so the sync is asserted by the suite instead of asked for in a comment.

**All seven, not the four that failed, and `:root` only.** Seven lets the call site read the token unconditionally rather than branch on which faction it drew; the three that already cleared only get better (coven 6.09 → 7.25, snide 7.07 → 15.84, wow 8.93 → 11.84). And the next editor's instinct on any `--faction-*` token is to add a `[data-theme="dark"]` override, which re-opens the bug on whichever half of the split gets the light hue — the declaration says so, and `factionContrast.test.ts` fails if anyone does it anyway. The other tempting shape, wrapping the sky in a nested `[data-theme="dark"]`, was worse than it looked at the time: a nested `[data-theme]` froze root-only aliases inside it, so it dragged every token those components read into audit scope to fix one numeral. #1839 fixed that mechanism (see above) and the objection no longer holds — but the ruling stands on its own, because a wrapper still flips *everything* underneath to fix one numeral.

`default` (na / albescent) has no `-on-night`, on purpose. The sky hands unaffiliated points no colour at all and lets `.rainbow-ink` clip them (ADR-0039); minting one would invite a caller to drop the `isKnownFaction` branch that keeps the spectrum.

### A spine hue is a FILL colour, and "theme-correct" is not "legible" (#1932)

The section above ends with the Constellation retired, and the note that replaced `SKY_PAIRS` in `factionContrast.test.ts` said the Players page "draws on the ordinary page ground, where the ordinary faction hue is the theme-correct ink". That sentence is the bug, written down. The rebuilt page then printed `--faction-{slug}` as text in six slots — the podium rank numeral and level label on both form factors, the roster's faction line, and `LevelGem`'s numeral through `--gem-ink` — and the first nightly to walk the page returned **fourteen measured failures, every one in light**: `--faction-wow` at 1.96:1 on `--color-bg-page` and 2.09:1 on the frost a signed-in player's own roster row wears, `--faction-ua` at 4.46:1 as an 18px/400 numeral. Dark cleared everything, 4.87 to 15.35.

**Flipping with the cascade is what #1792 asked of a hue, and it is all it asked.** That fix's whole point was that a token which does *not* flip cannot sit on a canvas that does not flip either, and the correction was to reach for the hue that does. Read one issue later, "the theme-correct ink" sounds like a clearance. It is a statement about *which of two values you get*, and says nothing about either one's ratio. **When a defect sorts perfectly by cascade it is one bug — and here the cause is that the light half of the pair was only ever a fill.**

**The declaration already carried the measurement, from the other side.** `--faction-wow-on-fill: #14110b; /* #e0a800 — white only 2.15:1; ink 8.76:1 */` — quoted as it read at the time; #2068 made that hue a plum and the row now says the opposite, which is the habit working rather than expiring. An `-on-fill` comment is the same arithmetic seen from the far side of the pairing: it exists *because* someone put the hue next to white and found 2.15. So the diagnostic habit is cheap — before inking anything in a spine hue, read that hue's `-on-fill` line. If the ink there is near-black, the hue is light enough that near-white will fail; if it is near-white, the hue is dark enough to work and the question is only by how much.

**Compute the ceiling before hunting a hex.** Clearing 4.5:1 on the frost needs an ink of relative luminance ≤ 0.1774. WOW's gold sits at 0.4385, so a passing colour on its own hue line is around `rgb(148, 111, 0)` — a bronze — and `--faction-wow-on-fill` would stop clearing on the fill it was measured for. There is no yellow, and that is arithmetic rather than a failure of search. So the honest fix is at the call site: **the hue keeps the fills, rules, rings, blooms, bars, strokes and glows, and the type reads a text tier.** UA's near-miss is the same call for a different reason — 4.46:1 against 4.59:1 as declared, the whole 0.13 being `#fcf7ef` versus the page's slightly darker `#f7f4ee` — and the 2026-08-14 owner ruling on #1766 is that a shortfall that small buys a site fix, not a per-faction on-page ink tier.

Two things this leaves behind.

**`--gem-ink` is the shape to recognise: an ink one indirection from the property that paints it.** `LevelGem`'s docstring has said since #728 that the gem is "outlined rather than filled … so the numeral sits on the surface the gem lands on … without per-faction contrast tuning", and the code then handed the numeral the faction hue — the exact tuning the outline exists to make unnecessary. Nothing named `color` was wrong at the call site, so a reader scanning for inks walked past it. It is `currentColor` now, which is what the docstring promised and what makes a future mount inside a faction frame correct without this component learning about it.

**And `local/no-global-ink-on-faction-surface` (#1819) could not have caught this, in two independent ways.** Its path scope is `archetypes/`, `mobileArchetypes/` and `components/factionMarks/`, and `pages/players/` is in none of them — correctly, because the Players page is neutral chrome that merely *dispatches* on a slug, and widening the glob would ban the global tiers on the surface they are right for. But even inside the scope the rule has the opposite polarity: it bans a global ink on a faction sheet. **The label tier's `--label-ink` seam is guarded in one direction only** — nothing stops an inline faction hue painting over it on a neutral sheet, which is what all three label sites did. The inverse rule is real work with its own legacy list; the cheap standing guard meanwhile is a DOM-less assertion at the mount, `pages/players/__tests__/playersFactionInk.test.tsx`, in the shape `composerQuietInk.test.tsx` already set. (**#2077 built the inverse rule** — `local/no-faction-hue-as-ink`. Read the subsection below before reaching for the glob: widening this one is the fix that looks obvious and is wrong twice over.)

### And the mirror of it: an ink that does not flip needs a GROUND that does not flip (#2133)

#1792 above is a token that flips laid on a canvas that does not. Run it backwards and you get the Everymen faction page, where the "Recent Praxis" heading was reported unreadable: `--everymen-ink` is a near-black in *both* cascades, because the stocks it was minted for — the cream board, the gold — are themselves theme-invariant. `.em-backdrop` is not. It ramps `--everymen-paper` to `--everymen-paper-deep` and both flip, so the heading measured **13.19:1** by day and **1.16:1** at night (1.06:1 at the deep end). The paired ink that flips with the stock, `--everymen-paper-text`, was already declared and already measured; the repaint is one word per call site and moves nothing in light, because the two tokens are the *same hex* there.

**A bug with no light mode survives every audit that is not run twice.** That identical light hex is why this outlived a full prod visual audit and a design review: there is nothing to see by day, and the two names are interchangeable right up to the moment the theme flips. The tell is available without rendering anything — **when an ink and its ground disagree about whether they flip, one of them is wrong, and which one is decided by the ground.** The Everymen family had already written this down four times in its own archetypes ("NOT `--everymen-ink`, which vanishes on the dark sheet"), one file at a time, which is the per-symptom fix this section keeps naming — four archetypes each learned it privately and none of them looked sideways. The sweep that found the two files still doing it is one grep for the *pairing* (`color:` next to the frozen ink), not a page-by-page eyeball, and it is what turned a report about one heading into a fix on six more sites of a mobile home nobody had opened in dark.

**The guard has to be per-element where the file is mixed, and only there.** `factionContrast.test.ts` cannot express this as a ratio row — both tokens clear on the grounds their declarations name, which is the Part A / Part B split the file's own docstring states. So it is a source rule, in the shape #1793 and #1766 set. `EverymenFieldDesk` takes a whole-file ban because every ground it paints is the paper family and `CREAM` is only ever an ink there; `EverymenFactionBody` cannot, because eight of its nine `color: INK` sites are inside `PAPER_FRAME`, whose cream *is* invariant and where the frozen ink is correct. **A file that mounts two stocks gets a rule per element, not per file** — a blanket ban there would have "fixed" eight correct call sites.

### When no ink can clear the ground, the repair becomes an ORNAMENT (#2173)

S.N.I.D.E. is the section above run in the opposite cascade: `--faction-snide-acid` is theme-invariant, `.snide-backdrop` flips, and the faction page's section heading measured **16.33:1** at night and **1.03:1** by day. Everymen was unreadable at night; this one was unreadable by day, and the fix #2133 used — repoint the ink at the one that flips with the stock — **had no rung to step onto.** The arithmetic says so before you go hunting: the light wall's relative luminance is 0.8389, which puts every AA-clearing ink below L 0.1475, and acid green is a *light* colour, so every candidate at that luminance is a dark olive. `-acid-deep` is the nearest one already declared and it reaches 2.30:1.

So the ruling changes the ground, which is what this section has been saying all along, and then does one thing more: **it spends the change as a drawing rather than as an apology.** Acid keeps its acid and carries a photocopier-black plate — `--faction-snide-ink`, the ground every *other* acid-as-type site in the faction was already printing on — and on the light wall that plate reads as a **censor bar**, which is on-voice for a flyposted zine. The rule generalises as one line: **`--faction-snide-acid` never touches paper.** Acid as a *drawn* thing (a rule, a stripe, a fill, a border) is untouched; this is a rule about type.

**The ornament is single-theme, and that has to be written at the site or it gets filed as a bug.** In dark the plate and the wall are both near-black, so the bar dissolves and the heading is plain acid — identical to before the fix. Asymmetry is the correct outcome here, because the *pairing* is symmetric (16.33:1 in both cascades) and only its visibility is not. The failure mode to guard against is someone lightening the plate so the bar shows at night: that spends exactly the ratio the whole ruling rests on.

**Classify before editing, because "29 sites" was one site.** The issue listed ~29 acid-as-text call sites across thirteen files. Every one of them except the section heading already sat on a dark ground — `INK_PANEL`, `-note-bar`, `-slip-bar`, `-stamp-bg`, `-card-bg`, and the hero and profile pages, both of which were dark *at the time* and neither of which is any more (#2343, #2631) — so wrapping them a second time would have been ~28 no-op diffs with a real chance of a mistake in one. The same walk found the *other* half of the bug, which was a tier error rather than a hue one: `--faction-snide-card-muted` is the SLAB's quiet ink and reads 1.24:1 on the light wall, so the two empty states standing on the wall take `--faction-snide-wall-text` instead. `card-muted` stays correct on cards. **A list of line numbers in an issue is a snapshot, not a scope.**

### A frozen GROUND owes a frozen MARK, and the plate is the wrong tool there (#2364)

The same faction, one surface over, in the third of the four directions this pair of rules can run — and worth stating separately because the previous section's repair is actively wrong here. #2173 was a frozen ink on a ground that flipped, so the ground moved and grew a plate. The S.N.I.D.E. faction-page hero's medallion is the mirror: the disc is `--faction-snide-paper`, which by ruling has **exactly one declaration** (#2227, because six surfaces spend it as an ink), and the sigil and its 2px rim painted `--faction-snide` — the CHROME hue, which flips `#6fae00` → `#b6ff2e`. 2.41:1 by day and **1.07:1** by night, on the same cream both times. **Reaching for #2173's plate would delete the thing being fixed**: the disc is paper *by design*, which is what makes the medallion a slapped sticker rather than another censor bar. When the ground is the frozen half and it is frozen deliberately, the mark is what moves.

**A drawn mark owes 3:1 (WCAG 1.4.11), not 4.5:1 — decide against that floor, or you will reject a correct repair.** And the arithmetic picks the token before any hunting starts, exactly as it did in #2133: the stock's relative luminance is 0.8797, so every mark clearing 3:1 on it sits below **L 0.26**, and both acid cuts are *light* colours (0.337 and 0.815). Neither can ever sit on this stock — same finding as the light wall, one surface over. The press's own `--faction-snide-ink` is the family member that can, at 16.68:1 in both cascades, and it is what the three other S.N.I.D.E. surfaces drawing on this exact disc already shipped (the faction page's inverted monogram, the praxis-detail avatar tile, the profile laurel). Acid is not lost from the hero — the wordmark, the motto sticker, the torn strip and all three stat chits are still acid, each on the plate it owes.

**The guard is a pairing assertion, not a ratio, because the ratio is the symptom.** Both tokens are correctly declared, so the value manifest is green; the disc keeps its one declaration, so #2227's block is green; #2173's sweep looks for the alias `ACID` set as a `color` with no plate under it, and this call site never called the chrome hue acid. What the new block in `factionContrast.test.ts` asserts first is that **a mark on a frozen ground resolves to the same value in both cascades**, and only then measures it — the marks read off the component source, so the rows cannot go on measuring a token it stopped painting.

### "Always-dark" is a property of every ground a faction paints, not a sentence in a docstring (#2631, ADR-0085)

Five bug reports, five identical owner rulings, one missing rule. Six S.N.I.D.E. surfaces each asserted *"its `--faction-snide-*` tokens are identical in both themes"* in their own prose, and it stopped being true at #1023 and #2065 — `-wall` and `-note-paper` both flip. Every one of those six was then filed separately as "a dark island on a light page", and each was repaired separately (#2227, #2287, #2343, and two more in the 2026-08-24 grill). **When the same ruling arrives five times, the thing to write is not a sixth fix.**

**A faction with three registers needs all three named, or a new surface picks one by vibe.** ADR-0085 names S.N.I.D.E.'s: the WALL (`-wall`, `-note-*`, `-composer-*`, `-slip-*`) is the flipping ground and everything read on it; the PRESS (`-ink`, `-paper`, `-acid`, `-pink`) is pigment — inks, fills, and the small opaque objects printed with them; the SLAB (`-card-*`) is the black clipping pasted *on* the wall. The recurring defect is one substitution: a large ground taking the press or the slab. Everything else in the faction was already right.

**A `data-theme` pin is worse than a wrong token and must be checked separately.** It freezes every alias inside a container to one cascade, so no token choice below it can be right — the sixth S.N.I.D.E. surface was unfixable from the inside for that reason, and its docstring explained the pin as a *consequence* of the premise that had stopped being true. Exactly one kit in the app may carry one: Singularity, whose ground is `#050f08` in both cascades because a terminal is black. **The guard for it is a whole-tree sweep, not a per-file one** — the next pin will not be in the files you are looking at.

**A mark that brings its own stock does not re-measure when the page changes stock.** That is what let the ransom slip, the motto sticker, the badge medallion and the mugshot travel through all six repairs untouched at 15.55:1, while every ink standing *straight on* the ground had to be read again. Sorting a surface's marks into "carries its own ground" and "reads the page's" before touching anything is what keeps a re-measurement from becoming a redraw.

### A frozen ground owes a frozen INK too, and `-on-fill` is not one (#2635)

The fourth direction, and the one that reads as already-solved right up until you measure it. #2364 froze the ground and moved the mark; this is the same shape with type, and the trap is that **the obviously correct token is the wrong one.** Two card masthead bands were grounded on paint borrowed from something else — UA's on `--faction-ua-hair`, "the faintest divider, below -rule" by its own declaration's comment, and Coven's on `--faction-coven-slip-sigil-ground`, the white disc that backs the *sigil*. The owner's ruling freezes UA's band at the faction's day orange in both cascades, mirroring `--faction-everymen-bill-mast` and `--faction-wow-plum-surface`, which have to be literals for the reason #1792 gives: the spine hue flips, so `var(--faction-ua)` is not "orange in both modes", it is two oranges.

**Then the ink. `--faction-{key}-on-fill` looks like the answer and is not, because the fill flips and the ink flips with it.** UA's is `#fcf7ef` by day and `#201a14` by night — a near-black, minted against the *dark* spine's `#e8703a`, where a warm white is 2.89:1. Pointed at a frozen orange it prints 3.52:1, under the floor, and inverts the type the ruling asked for. `-on-fill` is the ink for the fill, not for anything that happens to be the fill's colour. So the band mints `--faction-ua-mast-ink` beside `--faction-ua-mast`, which is exactly what `--faction-everymen-bill-mast-ink` is beside its own frozen red and why WOW letters its banner in the theme-invariant `--faction-wow-gilt-mid` rather than in `-stamp-total`, an ink that flips. **One frozen pair, one measurement, both cascades** — 4.59:1 here, a deliberate drop from the 10.35:1 the band paid on the hairline, because the band's job is to be the faction's colour rather than its quietest surface.

**And the mark, again — but check that the prop you pass arrives.** A faction's own sigil disappears on a ground of the faction's own hue, which is what `CardMasthead`'s `markColor` exists for. UA's `markColor` reached nothing: `UaSigilAdapter` destructured `{ size }` only, under a comment claiming the ensō "has no color prop", which stopped being true when #908 made it a mask with a `color` that *defaults* to the ornament glow. A prop that type-checks, is accepted, and is dropped is invisible to every guard in the repo — including the one comparing the dispatcher against the adapter, since both dropped it identically. **When a design says "override the mark's ink" and nothing changes, suspect the adapter before the token.**

**The standing rule the guard encodes: a band's ground is a token named for the band, or for a material the kit actually draws.** `-mast`, `-note-bar`, a plate's disc, a terminal's chrome, a plum banner — never a hairline and never the ground behind a mark. Both borrowed grounds here were perfectly *legible*, so a contrast sweep would have passed them forever; what was wrong is that nobody chose them, they were merely nearest to hand. The other five bands were left alone for the mirror reason: reading "the ground is not the faction's hue" as the bug flattens seven kits into one and turns S.N.I.D.E.'s note bar acid green. **A ground is a decision, and the guard has to be on the name.**

### A mark passed NO ink takes the page's, and the failure is different for every faction (#2723)

The section above is about an ink that was passed and dropped. This is its mirror: four `FactionSigil` mounts on the app's **neutral** chrome — the players race lane, the roster's faction column, and both of the sidebar's sigil rows — passed no `color` at all, and a mount that passes nothing does not get one answer, it gets **nine**. Each sigil component defaults to whatever its own file chose. The Ephemerists kite defaults to `currentColor`, which on neutral chrome is the *page's body ink*, so the kite drew near-black by day and **white by night**. S.N.I.D.E.'s defaults to `--faction-snide-acid` and WOW's to `--faction-wow-plum-surface`, both declared exactly once — theme-invariant by design, correct on the kit's own card, and each blind to one half of the cascade out here. Coven's and Singularity's happened to default to the spine hue and were right by accident. **One report ("the kite is white") was one symptom of a class with three different shapes in it, and the issue's own root cause named the wrong number of mounts.**

The answer at a neutral mount is `factionCssVar(slug)` — the bare spine hue, which is a FILL and so allowed here (§3 bans it as *ink*, and an SVG `fill` on a mark is not type). It carries both cascade halves, and on the race lane it is the token the lane's own bar already reads, so mark and bar become one colour instead of two. **Never a `-metal-*` / `-paper` / `-acid` material token on neutral chrome**: `--faction-ephemerists-metal-gold` is `#dcbb5e` in both themes and measures **1.69:1** on the page in light — a kit's materials assume the kit's ground. And the call must be **guarded**: `isKnownFaction(slug) ? factionCssVar(slug) : undefined`, the filter facet's shape from #2528, because `na` and `albescent` both map to CSS key `default` and that flat grey paints over the two spectra they own — the unaffiliated rainbow and the labyrinth's deliberate lack of livery. `AlbescentSigilAdapter` *forwards* `color` onto its mask, so unguarded this is a silent repaint of a secret society's only mark. **Neither standing guard can see any of this**: `local/no-faction-hue-as-ink` and `inkSeam.ts` both read `color`-role *declarations*, and a mark's ink is an `fill=` **attribute**. The probe that can is `utils/__tests__/sigilInk.tsx`.

### The fill-as-ink hides best in a SHARED helper, where no call site names a colour (#951)

The section above found the spine hue used as text in six slots of one page, and every one of them was visible: a reader scanning `pages/players/` for inks would have seen `--faction-{slug}` sitting in a `color`. The faction-card surface has the same defect in a shape that scanning cannot reach. `StatusBadge` and `InvitationNote` in `components/factionCard/FactionCard.tsx` take a **slug**, not a colour, and resolve `factionCssVar(slug)` internally — so the seven call sites read `<StatusBadge status={status} slug="wow" />` and name nothing at all. The hue is chosen by whoever mounts the helper, and the defect is per-faction: WOW's gold measures **1.96:1** on its own cream card ground in light mode and **11.19:1** in dark, so it fails on exactly the cards whose ground is pale, in exactly one theme.

**A helper parameterised by slug multiplies one line by the number of factions, and it does not report which of them are wrong.** That is the reason the fix here was scoped to one card rather than to the helper: WOW's muster bill (#951) draws its own standing plate and summons strip on `--faction-wow-plum-surface` / `-on-plum` at 5.16:1, and the shared branch is left in place with the measurement written beside it for whoever owns all six cards at once. Redrawing the helper means measuring six grounds against six hues, which is a different job with a different diff — but leaving the *note* on the branch is what stops the next card inheriting it silently.

**A negative guard is the cheap standing check, and its token string must be exact.** `components/factionCard/__tests__/wowMusterBill.test.tsx` asserts that `var(--faction-wow)` never appears as a colour in the rendered card, for every standing including both API aliases. The closing paren is load-bearing — eighty `--faction-wow-*` tokens share the prefix, and a guard matching the prefix would fail on the card's own correct inks. The obvious future edit ("why does WOW duplicate the badge? use the shared one") is what the guard exists to catch, because that edit is green and looks like cleanup.

### The rule that catches this is the INVERSE of #1819, not a wider glob on it (#2077)

The two sections above each ended with the same deferral: the inverse rule is real work, so a hand guard at the mount will do for now. #2077 is that work, and it arrived as the third instance — the Ephemerists brass, which #2068 dropped into the slot WOW's gold had been failing, at **2.19:1** on the page. Twelve inline sites: three in `FactionCard`, the metatask chip on `TaskCard`, both invitation rows, and **all seven** of the eight task-detail bylines that were painting a hue (S.N.I.D.E. already routed its own `PINK_INK`, which is the tell that the fix was known and applied once).

**Widening `no-global-ink-on-faction-surface`'s path list to `components/factionCard/` and `pages/` is the fix the issue asked for, and it is wrong twice over.** It cannot see a `--faction-*` token at all — its whole vocabulary is `var(--color-text-*)` — and on those two paths it would ban `var(--color-text-primary)`, *the token the fix reaches for*. The paragraph above had already recorded half of that for `pages/players/` ("widening the glob would ban the global tiers on the surface they are right for") and it holds identically for a faction card's generic branch, which is neutral chrome with a faction border. **When a rule's polarity is inverted relative to a defect, its scope is not the knob.**

`local/no-faction-hue-as-ink` therefore has **no path glob at all**, and that is the cleaner half of the design: the tier arm needs a list because the neutral tiers are genuinely *correct* on neutral chrome, whereas a bare spine hue is a FILL on every surface in the app and there is no ground where it becomes an ink. One rule, one claim, nothing to keep in step with the filesystem.

Three things worth carrying.

**It matches the HELPER CALL and not the token text, which is why eleven of the twelve sites were invisible to a `var()`-matching rule.** They are written `factionCssVar(slug)` — a `CallExpression`, containing the string `--faction-` nowhere. **Arity is the whole test**: a second argument names a shape (`'light'`, `'border'`, `'card-text'`) and those are measured pairings `CARD_PAIRS` has gated since #651, so a rule sweeping the prefix would report a measured pairing as a defect and teach the next editor to strip it. It also resolves a hue laundered through an in-module `const` — `const accent = factionCssVar(slug)` then `color: accent` — which is `--gem-ink`'s shape from #1932 and how five of the further sites it found are written. **A defect nobody named at the call site is the one a reader scanning for inks walks past.**

**A property NAME is not proof of a CSS role, and the one false positive is the useful part.** `editPraxis/roomPresence.ts` builds `{ color, colorLight }` for y-codemirror's awareness payload — a collaborator's **caret** and selection wash, not type — and takes the bare hue deliberately, because the mark lands on the viewer's editor ground rather than the remote's card sheet. It is exempted outright rather than grandfathered, for the reason the tier arm gives about `??` fallbacks: nothing there will ever migrate, and a shrink-only list holding a line nobody can delete is lying about the size of the debt. **Before listing a report as debt, check that the property is a CSS declaration and that the mark is type.**

**And the rule and the render guard are companions, not duplicates — each is blind where the other sees.** The rule reads source, so it stops the shape at the point of writing and reaches files no test mounts; it cannot follow a hue arriving as a prop, out of a `surfaceMap` dispatch, or from a slug-parameterised helper, which is exactly how `StatusBadge` hid this from seven call sites (#951). `src/__tests__/factionHueAsInkMounts.test.tsx` reads the emitted `style=` attribute and does not care how the value got there; it cannot reach a row behind a `useState(false)` toggle, which is where `pages/Factions.tsx` keeps its invitation panel — #694's "the surface is skinned by a faction is not the same claim as the sweep has been there", arriving at a click this harness cannot perform. Both halves read one shared `utils/__tests__/inkSeam.ts`, because a second copy of `INK_PROPS` reports green over precisely the property nobody added to it.

**Which ink replaces the hue is a WASH question as often as a ground question.** Two of the twelve pair the hue's own 10–16 % self-tint (`--faction-{key}-light`) with the hue as text, and a wash made of the ink's own hue can only tighten the reading (#1302). Measured over `-card-bg`, `-card-muted` — the role-correct token, and the one the sibling `burned` badge in the same helper already used — drops to **4.02:1** for WOW and **4.40:1** for Everymen in light, from 4.76 / 5.09 bare. So the two washed marks take `-card-text` (worst 10.32:1 light, 9.83:1 dark across all eight keys) and the one unwashed badge takes `-card-muted` (worst 4.76:1). **The same mark, twice, with two different answers because one of them lies under a tint.** Seven of the eight task-detail bylines needed no ink named at all: their `eyebrow` / `.label-caption` base already carried the ink every sibling caption on that ground wears, so the fix was deleting the override. WOW is the exception and names `--faction-wow-accent-deep`, because its `QUIET` is type-only and its headers lie straight on a darker parchment field that `-card-muted` does not clear.

The four files the rule found outside the report went onto a shrink-only list with their measurements in its header, not fixed at the time: each was neutral chrome painting a faction name in the bare hue, and each was also a visible repaint of a page #2077 did not rule on. One of them (`DefaultProposeTask`) argued for itself in prose — "the faction accent stays — ink is measured separately" — and reversing a written decision on the strength of a rule one commit old is the one-at-a-time failure this section names from the other side.

**That list is gone (#2108), and the twelfth site is why it had to be.** The owner ruled the whole class shut rather than the one reported surface: all eleven listed violations fixed, `.eslint-legacy-faction-hue-ink.txt` deleted, and the `length > 0` guard arm deleted with it — an empty ratchet file reads like debt nobody has looked at. Every one of the eleven was the same move and the prose the list quoted holds exactly as written: **the accent stays, only the type moves.** The twelfth site is the one that matters for the guard. `feedRowSkin`'s `actor` default was `factionCssVar(slug)` under a docstring promising the hue "is legible on the app's neutral page" — true of the pre-#2068 hue set, false of this one, and **a comment asserting a measurement that no longer holds is worse than no comment**, because the next reader takes it as verified. The rule could not see it: the key is `actor`, not `color`, and the value crosses a module boundary before it becomes a `color`. Two rungs were considered. Flagging every `factionCssVar(slug)` call with no shape argument — the general form, and genuinely the smell — reports **28 files**, and all but this one are correct FILLS (gradients, rings, gem glows, tints, colour bars); a shrink-only list of 28 correct files is debt-shaped noise. So the rule instead **names the far end**: `actor` joins `--gem-ink` in `INK_PROPS`, on the identical reasoning — it *is* a `color`, one indirection away, and the indirection is what hid it. **A cheap guard that names the far end beats a general one that needs a list of correct code to stay quiet.** The general form's upgrade path is type-aware linting, and it is written down at the rule.

### The rule was right and the vocabulary was short: `-accent-ink` (#2619)

Three sections above end with the same move — *the accent stays, only the type moves* — and the type always moves to a **neutral**. That is correct and it is also a loss, stated as such by the surface that finally could not pay it. #2298 ruled that an invitation letter's real perk takes the faction accent on its name so the true mechanic reads first; the rule blocked every spelling of it, and what shipped was `--color-text-primary` with the flavour names dropped to tertiary. The intent survived by *contrast* rather than by hue. **The gap was never the rule. It was that there was nothing legitimate to point at** — `-card-text` / `-card-muted` are measured against a faction *sheet* (near-white on S.N.I.D.E. and Singularity, whose cards are dark-in-light), and `--color-text-*` is the absence of the answer rather than the answer.

`--faction-{key}-accent-ink` is that answer: **the accent, measured safe as ink on the page**, reached as `factionCssVar(slug, 'accent-ink')`. §3's claim is untouched and still exactly true — *the hue is never an ink*. This is a different token that **aliases** the hue wherever the hue already clears, and an alias is the honest expression rather than a shortcut: the entire dark cascade is one (na 5.53:1 to snide 15.35:1 on `#13121a`), plus wow and everymen in light. **A family that costs four values instead of nine is the measurement talking, not a compromise.**

**Two grounds, because a token's stated ground is not always the one it lands on.** The family's ground is the page — but its first consumer, the letter's perk box, lays `--faction-{key}-light` over that page, and §3 already knows what a self-tint does: *a wash made of the ink's own hue can only tighten the reading* (#1302). Values chosen on bare paper did not survive their own mount — ua fell from 4.51:1 to **3.83**, coven and singularity to **4.12**, na to **4.26** — so each value clears 4.5:1 on the **tint**, the tighter of the two, and therefore clears the page with room (4.72 to 5.80). Both grounds are rows in `factionContrast.test.ts`; the page rows alone go green on inks that are illegible where they actually print. **Measure the ground the token lands on, then the ground it is named for.**

**Two factions pay for this in character, knowingly.** Darkened far enough to clear, snide reads as a dark olive rather than an acid green and ephemerists as a muddy bronze rather than plate brass — the same place `--faction-wow-accent-deep` and Coven's stamp gold had to land, and for the same reason: *some hues have no value on near-white paper that is both legible and still themselves*. That was accepted rather than overlooked. The fallback, **for one faction alone and not for the family**, is the neutral ink this shipped instead of.

**A margin of 0.01 is not a margin.** The proposal for ua was a single lightness step to a nominal 4.51:1; re-measured against the real token it was **4.49** — under the floor before it ever met the tint. Two lessons, both cheap: **re-measure a proposed hex rather than pasting it**, and **a value that clears by hundredths is broken by the next nudge to the page ground**, so take the step that buys room.

### Contrast is a pairing, not a property (#1028)

A measurement belongs to a **hue on a ground**. It survives exactly as long as both halves do, and the task-detail epic broke that in two ways that each turned up independently on three separate skins.

**A new ground invalidates every contrast claim measured on the old one.** Everymen, WOW and Ephemerists each hit this without knowing the others had: a token that had cleared AA for releases stopped clearing the moment its surface was repainted, and nothing warns you — the token is unchanged, so it looks untouched. Ephemerists is the worked example. A task *card* is one plate sitting on the app's page ground; a task *detail* **is** the plate, so it gained a page ground of its own and a second sheet for the panel cells, and it sets quiet type on all three. The design's `muted` brown clears 4.60:1 on the plate and only 4.31:1 on the page beneath it. The fix is not to branch per ground at the call site — it is to walk the ink down until it clears on **every** ground that surface has (`--faction-ephemerists-plate-quiet`: page 5.25, plate 5.60, inner 6.20 on the papyrus this was measured on; 6.38 / 5.98 / 5.52 since #1627 took the register dark), so a skin never has to know which sheet a label landed on. **When a surface gains a sheet, re-measure the inks it already had.**

**A sheet can also arrive as a PANEL, and a shared slot is where you find out (#1173).** The rule above assumes the surface was repainted. It applies just as hard when nothing changed and the skin merely inset a plate: every duel seal mounts `StakesTiles` and `RaceRoster` on a deeper stock than its body ground and hands them `theme.muted`, an ink measured on the *sheet*. Two of the eight sat under AA there — WOW's `--faction-wow-card-muted` at **4.24:1** on the chronicle panel, `--everymen-muted` at **4.25:1** on `--everymen-paper-deep` — while both cleared on the body ground the whole time (4.76 / 5.09). So `-quiet` is now the standing name for *same role, second ground*: `--faction-wow-chronicle-quiet` (4.86) and `--everymen-quiet` (4.77) join `--faction-ephemerists-plate-quiet`, each a **sibling** that leaves the original ink every job it already had. (Everymen has since spent this three times over, once per stock, and the family is the worked example of where it ends: `--everymen-red` keeps every band, frame and rule, while `-sheet-accent` inks the broadsheet panel, `-paper-accent` the paper — #1341 — and `-deep-accent` the deep tan the mobile field desk stamps its chits out of, at 3.75:1 in the bare red, #1766. **Count the stocks a family prints text on; that is how many accent inks it owes.**) Reaching for some other existing ink at the call site is the tempting shortcut and it does not survive the third faction: `-card-notice` clears on WOW's plate and Everymen's panel and fails the Ephemerists band at 4.43 — and it would recolour the tie line and the tile captions, which are muted-role, not a notice. **A named ink per (role, ground) beats a per-skin exception.**

**And the third stock can be a PAGE whose real ground is a composite nobody wrote down (#2248).** WOW spent the `-quiet` split above on its parchment panel and then set section heads, links and empty-state copy straight onto the pavilion — `wowMobilePage` / `.wow-backdrop`, which paint a court glow and a gilt hatch over a cream sweep. No token holds the colour those three layers add up to. Where they cross it is **`#dcc6a1`**, and every chronicle ink except the body ink misses there: `-card-accent` 3.82:1, `-accent-deep` 3.51, `-card-muted` 3.14, all measured on the cream *card* and all still correct on it. Two things this pins. **Receding the ground is the first thing to try and it can be arithmetically impossible** — take the hatch and the glow to zero and the ground is still `-ground-to` #f1e3b8, where `-card-muted` reaches 4.09; only the ink side moves, so the walked siblings `--faction-wow-ground-accent` / `-ground-label` / `-ground-quiet` land at 4.66 / 4.61 / 4.61 on the crossing and 6.0+ on the stop. And **a light-only failure gets a light-only family**: the night pavilion composites to #32271c, where the same three card inks read 6.39 / 7.10 / 5.51, so the dark cascade aliases each sibling straight back rather than minting a night value nothing needed. **A ground made of layers is a ground; measure the composite, not the stop underneath it.**

**An accent can clear AA as a rule or a fill and still fail as body ink — and that is a token split, not a usage convention.** "Never paint text in this one" is a rule a future editor has to remember; a second token is a rule the cascade enforces. `--everymen-red` pays 4.95:1 on the light broadsheet panel and 3.73:1 on the dark one, so ink got its own name — `--faction-everymen-sheet-accent`, walked up in the dark cascade to 5.45:1 — while dashed rules and filled bars keep `--everymen-red`. WOW's gold and the WOW duel rail's opponent accent state the same constraint as a usage convention *because they genuinely have no text pairing*: nothing legible is ever painted in either. The moment a surface does want the accent as text, mint the ink token rather than writing the caution down.

**And an INK must not be a gradient stop either — the rule runs both ways (#1766).** The paragraph above is the accent side. `--faction-default-gold` is the other: it was declared `var(--faction-default-stop-3)`, and the comment beside it warned that *a gradient must not read it back, or a ramp ends up composed from a text colour*. The half nobody wrote down is that reading in the borrow's own direction is just as wrong. A ramp position is chosen for how it sits **between its neighbours**, and #ca8a04 has to be a yellow the eye reads as yellow between orange and green; asked to be type on the na score stamp's pure-white plate it paid **2.94:1**, and on the card sheet 2.89. It failed **all fourteen light combinations** of the nightly sweep on its own — the largest single contributor in the suite — for a reason worth generalising: this is `na`/default chrome, so every walked route paints it, where a faction hue fails on one faction's pages. **A defect in the default family costs seven times what the same defect costs in a faction's.** The stop is untouched (seven cuts read it); the ink is a literal now, walked to 5.00 / 4.92, and the price is that it no longer follows a re-cut of the spectrum. Say that at the declaration when you break an alias, because the next editor re-cutting the ramp is the person who needs to know.

The split it forced is the polarity rule below arriving at a token that is not a faction's: the same gold dressed **Singularity's** roster readouts on a terminal that is near-black in *both* themes. Two consumers, opposite grounds — and the band is provably empty rather than merely tight, since white wants luminance ≤ 0.183 and the terminal ≥ 0.191. So `--faction-singularity-amber` carries exactly what the alias resolved to in each cascade and nothing on that surface repaints. **When one ink serves two grounds of opposite polarity, splitting is not a judgement call, it is arithmetic** — and the split that changes no pixels is the cheap one to ship, because the whole diff is the coupling.

**A token correctly scoped in its own declaration can still be misused, and that is a guard, not a comment (#1766).** `--faction-ua-vermil` says *"the ensō score numeral — large display type only"*, and the contrast manifest measures it at the large-text 3:1 floor for exactly that reason. UA's mobile faction page then painted it on a 14px/600 member-row rank — 4.12:1 on the dark sheet, a real AA failure that every ratio in the manifest stayed green through, because **the pairing a manifest asserts is the one the DECLARATION describes and never the one a component drew**. The fix is one token (`--faction-ua-card-accent`, 5.18 / 6.58, measured on that ground since #848); the thing worth keeping is the guard beside it — a *reader allowlist*, in the shape #1449's non-ratio assertion and #1413's declaration check already set here. A scope written in a comment is a rule a future editor has to remember. A list the suite reads is one they cannot forget, and adding a reader is a line of diff that makes you say out loud that the numeral is at display size.

**A block whose ink you do not control gets the stock that ink was measured on (#1118).** The corollary of the first rule, and the one that decides a shared page's grounds. A skin that dresses a shared layout mounts components it did not write, and each arrived carrying its own inks: the vote summary paints `--color-text-secondary`, which FLIPS with the theme, so it needs a ground that flips with it; `SnideScoreStamp`'s evidence tag is acid on a `rgba(0,0,0,0.4)` well and `SnideVote`'s amp face is a black chassis, both measured on photocopier ink, and acid on cream paper is 2.6:1. The S.N.I.D.E. praxis detail therefore runs two grounds side by side — flipping clippings and an always-dark plate — and which block gets which is a measurement rather than a taste call. **Do not unify a rail's panels for tidiness; check what each mounted component's inks were measured against first.**

**But "you do not control it" is often just a missing seam, and the seam beats the ground (#1153).** `DuelCard` was the fourth block in that list until every one of the nine praxis-detail skins hit it independently — Coven, S.N.I.D.E., Singularity and UA each reported the same thing, that the card's frame and label were dressable and the duellist names, totals and verdict were not, so the most prominent slot in the aside sat in Default furniture inside a fully dressed page. The escapes available without a seam are all worse than a prop. Choosing the ground the foreign ink was measured on is correct but limited: it dresses the *stock* and leaves the *ink* someone else's, which is fidelity lost, not a contrast bug. Re-pointing the tokens for the subtree works — Singularity had to, being near-black in both themes where the shared component assumed light-in-light — but a re-point is invisible from the component's side and lands on **everything** underneath rather than the four slots that were meant. And a per-skin fork of the component is the divergence a shared layout exists to prevent. So: **when a shared component's ink is wrong on your ground, add the ink prop to the component once rather than working around it eight times** — defaulting every field to what it painted before, so a faction that passes nothing is byte-identical. The ground rule above survives intact; it decides where a block sits when the ink genuinely is not yours, which after a seam lands is a smaller set.

**And the ground a mounted component needs is not always a contrast question (#1119).** The last of the nine praxis-detail skins found the same rule binding through a different mechanism, which is worth naming because the failure looks nothing like a contrast failure. UA's rail plates are `--faction-ua-card-bg` and may not be `-lift` or `-panel`, for two reasons that arrive from opposite directions: `UaScoreStamp`'s box is `--faction-ua-card-box-bg`, which *lifts* off the sheet in light and *sinks below* it in dark, so a plate already painted in the lift erases the box in one theme only; and `UaVote`'s mandala punches its cores to `--faction-ua-card-bg` **so the figure reads as an aperture** — on any other stock the cutout stops being a hole and the bloom becomes a disc. Nothing here is illegible; the drawing is simply wrong. So the question to ask of a mounted component is wider than "what were its inks measured on": it is **what did it assume it was sitting on**, and a punched-out core, a nested well or a shadow calibrated to one sheet all answer it as firmly as a contrast ratio does.

The third variant of this — a design kit's own annotations measured against the kit's plate rather than ours — is §6's, under "Reading a design kit".

**A SHARED faction-themed component owes its inks to eight sheets, not to the app (#694).** The two shapes above are about one surface; this is about one component mounted on all of them, and it fails in two ways that look nothing alike until you measure.

The first is a **token used in the wrong role**. `--faction-{slug}-card-muted` is muted *text* — every caller in the app reads it as `color:` — and the collab roster's cast row read it as `background:`. That renders as a slab of ink with the accent pill printed on top: **1.05:1 in light, 1.17:1 in dark**, the worst pairing yet measured in this repo, and the member's own name at 1.99:1 beside it. The role of a token is not a comment on it, so nothing catches this; the guard against it is that only one member of the card family is a surface. `card-bg` is the sheet, `light` is a tint wash, and everything else — `-text`, `-muted`, `-accent`, `-notice`, `-credit` — is ink. A cast row therefore fills with `card-bg` and sets its own `card-text`, which has the second virtue of making the rendered pairing identical to one `CARD_PAIRS` has gated since #651. **A row that paints its own ground must paint its own ink**; an inherited colour is whatever the mount happened to set, and a shared component has several mounts.

The second is a **global functional ink on faction paper**. `--color-warning` and `--color-success` were chosen against the app's near-white surface and are correct there. On UA's cream the warning is 4.14:1 as the "still weaving" pill and 3.98:1 under the holdout banner's amber veil; on S.N.I.D.E.'s near-black card the success green is 2.07:1. The fix is not to deepen the global token — that repoints a colour eight other surfaces read as a fill and a rule, and it cannot work anyway: **these inks flip on the SHEET's polarity, not on the theme.** Six card sheets are light by day and dark by night, but S.N.I.D.E.'s and Singularity's are dark in both (§6), so no single light-theme value clears both `#f7e7d2` and `#14110b`. That is the shape that forces a per-faction token rather than a global one, and it is worth recognising early: if a colour must differ between two factions *within one theme*, it is a faction token no matter how functional the word for it sounds. Hence `--faction-{slug}-card-notice` and `--faction-{slug}-card-credit` — new members of the card ink family, measured against `--faction-{slug}-card-bg` in both themes.

**A veil is part of the pairing.** The holdout banner lays `rgba(234,179,8,0.08)` over the sheet before the ink lands, which pulls the ground *toward* the ink on a light sheet and away from it on a dark one — the veiled reading is the tighter one in both directions, so it is the one that gates. `Pair` in the token test now takes a `veil`, because a manifest that can only ask "is this ink legible on that sheet" asks the wrong question the moment anything is laid over the sheet.

**Neither guard could have caught any of it, and the reasons differ.** The token test measures a token against the surface its *documentation* names, and `--color-warning` documents no faction surface — the pairing was not merely missing from the manifest, it was unrepresentable in it. The nightly sweep walks six read routes, and the composer is on none of them; worse, the failing row only exists in a *state* (two or more members, some but not all cast) that no route walk produces. **"The surface is skinned by a faction" is not the same claim as "the sweep has been there"** — before trusting either guard on a surface, check that the route is walked and that the state is reachable.

**The second instance found the same ink family on sixteen more surfaces, and the reason it went unfound is the useful part (#1168).** UA's duel seal skin measured `--color-danger` at 3.98:1 on its own cream and routed its forfeit body to `-card-notice`, keeping the red as a rule beside the paragraph — a correct fix, made **one skin at a time**, which is the failure mode this whole section exists to name.
