**And "motion alone" is now narrowed a second time: a MARK may differ too, for a viewer who has already been let in (ADR-0088, #2731).** Every rule above — the delta is motion, na keeps the words, Albescent adds no hue — was written for a surface a *stranger* might be reading. ADR-0082 replaced secrecy with redaction, and the question that leaves is about the viewer rather than about the paint: on a surface that identifies a **player**, `isFactionRedacted()` decides which dress is drawn. Unrevealed is na's, byte for byte; revealed gets Albescent's labyrinth on the avatar's corner badge. Three things keep this from being the livery #783 forbids. **It is a shape, not a hue** — `AlbescentSigil` is filled with `--faction-default-rainbow-conic`, the same spectrum na's ring sweeps, and `CSS_KEY.albescent` stays `"default"`. **It is gated, so it renders to nobody who was not already told** — which was the real objection in `AlbescentAvatar`'s old docblock ("it would render to every viewer at every size"), and the gate answers it where a size threshold could not. And **it is static**, so #2502's 64px ring gate is untouched: that gate is an argument about *motion* in a column of forty discs, and a still badge is not a spotlight. The pattern to copy on the next player-identifying surface is the avatar's: give the na component the seam it lacks (`DefaultAvatar` grew a `glyph` slot beside its `ornament` one), fill it from the wrapper behind the existing predicate, and add no second predicate of your own.

**A gradient border is a MASK, and this file has never once reached for `border-image`.** `border-image` does not clip to `border-radius`, which is the trap and the reason there is not one occurrence of it in `index.css`. Both local techniques draw the gradient and punch the middle out, so **there is no solid border colour in either and nothing needs a `border-color` fallback** — the other half of ADR-0039's "a `border: Npx solid` cannot hold a gradient" (§ above): it cannot, so do not try, ring it instead. Two rings exist and they are not interchangeable. `mask-composite: xor` on an absolutely positioned `::before` needs nothing from the element but a corner, so it is the one for a sheet a component paints inline; `.alb-detail-ring`'s pseudo-rim with a real `-face` child punched over it needs a face element to punch *with*, so it is for a disc with one number in it. Note what that docblock records and why it generalises: the rim is a pseudo-element because **spinning the element itself would rotate the content inside**. Around a panel of copy that is decisive — so the drift is a `background-position` slide on the pseudo-element and nothing the reader is reading moves at all. **Reach for the tiling cut whenever a ramp wraps a corner or travels:** `--faction-default-rainbow-loop` repeats its first colour at 100%, so no red-meets-magenta seam ever crosses the eye; plain `-rainbow` does not tile and `-rainbow-conic` is for a disc.

**Census before you mount "everywhere" — most of it may already be built (#2404).** "All albescent things" sounded like six new mounts. It was two: `AlbescentTaskCard`, `AlbescentTaskDetail`, `AlbescentPraxisDetail`, `AlbescentFeedFrame` and `AlbescentProfileBody` had each shipped a drifting spectrum edge already, and the desktop praxis card was the only ordinary surface without one. The three that stayed untouched were the **member-only** surfaces — the invite letter, the `/factions` tile, the metatask seal — which painted a private register rather than the na sheet, so a na rainbow on them would have been a repaint of the wrong thing. **That second set no longer exists (#2632):** the register is deleted, all three wear na's clothes, and the census question is the ordinary one again. The finding the paragraph was written for survives the change — *"every Albescent surface" is a claim to COUNT, not to assume* — and it is now the count that matters rather than the split.

**A shared class plus one CSS variable beats a conditional class threaded through three panels.** The rail's frame is unconditional markup: every panel carries `.spectrum-frame`, and a *member's* ring is switched off by `--spectrum-frame-image: none`, declared once in `railFaceVars` alongside the eight locals it already sets, so their own hairline stands and no panel needs to know who is looking. The drift is one attribute on the `<aside>` — the same shape as `data-rail-face` — because a custom attribute reaches every panel by descent where a class would have to be passed down. **And the motion lives in the deferred `motion.ornament.css`, never the render-blocking sheet:** colour and layout may never defer, motion is exactly what that sheet is for, and the rule there declares no keyframe of its own — `alb-drift` was already built for `.alb-rainbow` at the same 220px tile, keyframe names are document-global, and the entry sheet always loads. The panel keeps its own hairline *under* the ring, so a sheet whose spectrum reads faint against the page still reads as a panel. The whole ruling cost **60 bytes** of critical CSS.

**Six copies of one rule cost RAW bytes, not gzipped ones — measure before you promise headroom (#2407).** Five hand-rolled Albescent spectrum edges collapsed onto `.spectrum-frame::before`'s selector list, and the four byte-identical `@keyframes` behind them retired into one. Raw critical CSS fell **2,051 bytes**; gzip-9, which is the budget's unit, fell **122**. That is not a disappointment, it is how DEFLATE works: five verbatim repeats of the same 200-byte mask block are back-references costing a few bytes each, so *duplication of an identical string is nearly free after compression and duplication of a near-miss is not*. **Collapse repeated rules for the reason that they are one idea, and go hunting for novel bytes when the budget is the goal.** Two things about how to do the collapse safely. Per-site differences are plain overrides in the site's own rule — later source order at equal specificity — and a custom property is worth its indirection only where the value arrives from an ANCESTOR or from JS, which is why `--spectrum-frame-image` stays a variable and width/tile/opacity do not. And **`border-radius: inherit` reads the DIRECT parent, not the rounded thing nearby**: of the five edges only the praxis detail's and the profile band's are mounted straight inside the sheet they trace, so only those two could drop their hardcoded corner; the other three hang off an unrounded `.alb-task` / `.alb-detail` / `.alb-feed` wrapper where `inherit` resolves to `0` and would have squared the ring off.

**A background is a LIST in three properties at once, so a sheet token is a matched TRIPLE (#2497).** `--faction-default-card-sheet` never travels without `-sheet-blend` and `-sheet-clip`, and the three must declare the same number of layers, because **CSS cycles a short background list rather than padding it**. That is the failure mode to picture: na's sheet is one layer, so a lone `-sheet` token beside a hardcoded `background-blend-mode: multiply` is right by accident; the day a dresser's dark half arrives with five radials, the one blend value repeats across all five and the surface mis-composites — silently, and only at night. Two corollaries. The **background colour** is clipped by the list's *bottom-most* value, so a consumer writes `background-clip: var(--…-sheet-clip), border-box` and gets a sheet that stops at the padding box over a colour that runs to the edge — which is what a translucent dark hairline needs to show the sheet through. And a **surface with extra layers of its own** (the na task card's spectrum border) appends to all three lists rather than opting out, so its arity follows the sheet's however far the sheet grows. na declares today's exact paint, so "no visual change" is true by construction rather than by inspection.

**An inline style is the one place a stylesheet cannot reach — so shared PAINT takes a class and per-mount GEOMETRY stays inline (#2497).** The na spectrum was `background: var(--faction-default-rainbow)` written out at twenty-one mounts across the ten `Default*` files; every one rendered correctly and none of them could be dressed, which is why the Albescent design canvas reached for a DOM walk to animate them. `.spectrum-rule` (the linear cut — bands, hairlines, dividers) and `.spectrum-dial` (the conic cut — rings and dials) join `.spectrum-frame` and carry exactly one declaration each. **The split is paint versus geometry, not "everything the mounts have in common":** the profile band is 3px at 0.5 and the task-detail heads are 2px at 0.55, so height, opacity, radius and position stay at the call site and folding any of them into the class would repaint twenty other surfaces. Two things follow. **Mint the override variable only where something declares it** — `--spectrum-frame-image` exists because `railFaceVars` sets it to `none` for a member; the two siblings have no such declarer, so a dresser uses `.alb-x .spectrum-rule` and wins on specificity, and an unused `var()` name would fail `factionTokensDeclared` anyway. And a **conditional** ramp (the praxis detail's rung dots, painted per vote value) cannot become a class and is left alone rather than contorted.

**A SLUG WHOSE IDENTITY IS A GRADIENT FALLS BACK TO GREY EVERY TIME A SURFACE ASKS FOR A COLOUR, AND THE FALLBACK IS INVISIBLE IN REVIEW (#2730).** The players podium drew its rank ring from `factionCssVar(slug)` and its leader wash from `color-mix(… ${color} 10%, transparent)`, so `na` and Albescent — both CSS key `default` — got the flat `--faction-default` grey and, because `color-mix()` cannot take a gradient, no wash at all. The card's own docblock said this was covered ("the rank ring, the avatar and the points numeral already carry the spectrum"); only the numeral did. **A skip-it comment is a claim about a sibling, and a sibling is exactly what nobody re-reads** — the surviving half of that paragraph was true, which is what made the false half survive with it. Two shapes come out of the fix. **A scalar accent on a CIRCLE is `factionFill`'s `"frame"` cut conic**, not its 90deg one: the ramp goes on the border box, the neutral card paper on the padding box (a gradient border over a see-through interior shows the ramp straight through the middle, #794), and the numeral takes that paper's paired ink rather than the card's. The bloom beside it does not survive the swap, because a `box-shadow` spread takes a colour and a ramp is not one — so the ring is 2px where the hairline was 1, which is the stroke the avatar's own spectrum ring beside it already wears. And **`.spectrum-wash` is the family's fourth member and the one that is not a single declaration**, because a *fade* is a mask: `opacity` on the card would take the name and the points down with it, and a page-coloured veil over the ramp is an opaque rectangle that only looks right for as long as the mount has no background of its own. Pseudo-element, `opacity: 0.1`, `mask-image` for the fade, `z-index: -1` under `isolation: isolate` so the wash stays behind the copy and inside the card. The axis is the mount's (`--spectrum-wash-axis`, 170deg on the desktop column, 90deg on a row three times shorter) and the fade extent is not — one knob, not two, for a 6% difference in where a 10% tint reaches zero. **51 gzipped bytes**, which put the CSS budget over its warn line.

**DRESSING A SHARED SPECTRUM CLASS MEANS GROWING IT A PSEUDO-ELEMENT, AND WHICH RAMP THE CHILD TAKES DEPENDS ON WHETHER THE MOUNT'S OWN CUT CLOSES (#2501).** The score stamp is the first dresser #2497 anticipated, and the whole of `AlbescentScoreStamp` is `DefaultScoreStamp` plus `.alb-stamp` — **no ornament span at all**, because the two things that move were already drawn (the 2px `.spectrum-rule` band on its top edge, the 96px `.spectrum-dial` annulus round the total). That is the cheapest shape an unfreeze can take, and it is available wherever na already paints the thing the flourish wants to animate. Three rules generalise. **A mark that CONTAINS type turns its rim, never itself** — spinning `.spectrum-dial` would spin the total inside it — so the conic moves to a `::before` and the mount's real child is lifted to `position: relative`, because an absolutely positioned pseudo-element paints over a *static* sibling however late that sibling sits in the DOM. **A travelling child needs the ramp cut to close and an inherited one may not be**: `.spectrum-dial` rests on `--faction-default-rainbow-conic`, where two tiles of a wheel is a wheel, so `background-image: inherit` registers the rim exactly over the annulus and the deferred sheet arriving changes no pixel; `.spectrum-rule` rests on `--faction-default-rainbow`, whose last stop is **not** its first, so the child has to name `--faction-default-rainbow-loop` or put a violet-meets-red seam through every cycle. And **reuse the keyframe before minting one** — the band is `alb-edge-travel` (slide a two-tile child by one tile) and the ring is `alb-spin` (a bare 360deg turn, renamed from `alb-detail-spin` when it acquired a second consumer, the way `alb-wheel` was named in #2498). Four byte-identical keyframes under four surface-shaped names is the duplication #2407 and #2498 each spent a PR collapsing.

**A disclosure wrapper is not a plate, and mistaking one for the other repaints seven factions (#2497).** `SectionPanel` is the folded half of a faction page's section — an id and `hidden={!section.open}` — and all eight archetypes mount it. Putting the sheet there looks like the one-line way to give `DefaultFactionBody` the plate it never had; it would draw a *second* plate inside each of the seven bodies that already dress their own sections, and on the eighth it would fold the kicker away with the gallery it labels. The plate is `.faction-plate` on the `<section>`, outside the disclosure, with `.faction-plate-kicker` for the header row and `.spectrum-rule` for the hairline — so the plate's rule is the same ornament the cards' bands are and moves when they move. **When a shared component looks like the obvious mount, check who else mounts it before you paint on it.**

**A COMPOSER'S GROUND IS ALREADY SPENT, so the tell goes to the edge (#2505).** The design canvas draws Albescent's composer with a bloom behind the live textarea and flags it as the one place the ground might need dialling back. Measured on the composited ground rather than on the declared token — #2485's rule, and the fifth composer it catches — the dial-back is **to nothing**: `ComposerGround` washes the seven-stop aurora under the whole content column, and the na composer's quiet tier (`--faction-default-composer-faint`, which sets the autosave line, the inactive Write/Preview tab, `Save draft` and `Drop`) already reads **3.67:1 in light and 3.02:1 in dark** there, against flat-token readings of 4.86 and 4.61. So `AlbescentEditPraxis` adds no wash at all; its whole delta is `.alb-composer-edge`, a travelling spectrum ring on the sheet's perimeter, which is decorative chrome beside no type and owes no ratio. Two things generalise. **A composer is where people read their own words while typing, so legibility outranks the tell there** — more than on a card, which is read once. And **the ink at risk is the ink on the SHEET, not the ink in the field**: `--faction-default-composer-field` is opaque, so nothing the aurora does reaches inside the textarea, and a reading taken "behind the textarea" measures a ground that does not exist. (The na shortfall above is the #2485 family and is not Albescent's to fix; it is recorded here so the next composer dresser measures before it paints.)

**A TRAVELLING EDGE REPLACES A STATIC RULE — it never stands beside one (#2505).** The mobile field desk's identity block already carries the na spectrum as a 3px hairline (#1553's "one mark at three scales"), so an Albescent ornament span hung near it would have put a second spectrum on a band that has one. The whole wrapper is therefore a classed div and three declarations: `DefaultFieldDesk`'s hairline takes `.spectrum-rule`, and `.alb-desk .spectrum-rule` re-cuts the same ramp as the tiling loop with the shared travelling child. **Before mounting an ornament, look for the ornament the na surface already draws — the flourish is usually that mark coming alive, not a new one.** And the travelling child generalises past rings: `.alb-desk .spectrum-rule` is a **filled bar**, which cannot wear the `mask … exclude` idiom (it would punch its own middle out), but it wants exactly the same track — the loop cut, a 300% tile, `overflow: hidden`, and a pre-painted `::before` slid by `transform` from the deferred sheet. A filled bar and a masked ring are the same travelling problem, so neither needed a keyframe of its own. The price is that the rest frame shows a *third* of the ramp where the undressed band shows the whole of it: **rest and travel must show the same stripe width**, or switching motion on becomes a design change wearing a perf change's clothes.

### A faction's SPINE HUE and its SKIN are two different things (#812, #814, #838)

Warriors of Whimsy is the case that proves it, and the case that got it wrong twice.

WOW's **spine hue is plum** since #2068 and was **yellow** (#e0a800 / #f5c542) from #812 until then: `--faction-wow` is #7a4a9e light / #a875c9 dark. That token is its membership of the rainbow — index 5 of `FACTION_RAINBOW_ORDER` since #2078 re-sorted it, index 2 while it was a yellow — and it is why `isKnownFaction('wow')` is true and its members get faction-coloured ornament.

**The swap is the strongest possible proof that the two axes are independent, because it did not move the skin at all.** Seven distinct hues went in and seven came out: WOW vacated the yellow slot, the Ephemerists took a gold (#c9a24b / #e6c877, the plate's own brass) and the teal slot emptied. Not one card, page, register or `--faction-*-plate-*` token moved, which is only possible because the spine was never doing that work. Four things the swap settles, and they are the ones to read before touching a spine hue again.

**A spine hue is a fill, so the fill's own ground is what should choose it.** #1932's arithmetic said there was no yellow that could be read as text on the app's page, and #951's said the same about WOW's cream card — 1.96:1 on both. The answer was never a better yellow; it was to stop asking a yellow. The same token now reads 5.80 on the page and 5.79 on the cream, and Ephemerists' hue on its own near-black plate went 2.91 → 7.22. **A hue that keeps failing on its faction's own surfaces is a hue on the wrong faction**, and a swap costs nothing where every other faction's slot is equally arbitrary.

**`-on-fill` can flip the OTHER way, and then a missing dark row is a bug.** Every flipping row in that family had been white in light and ink in dark, because a lifted fill is brighter. WOW's pair now runs the reverse — white on the light plum (6.36:1, ink 2.96) and ink on the lifted one (5.43:1, white 3.47) — so its dark declaration is load-bearing rather than a restatement, while Ephemerists (ink on both golds) stopped needing one and had its dark row deleted. Read "omitted, so it inherits" as a *claim about a measurement*, not as tidiness.

**A borrowed `-on-fill` is where the swap actually bit, and only the manifest saw it.** WOW's skin had been inking its golds — the writ's submit button, the duel seal's champion tile — with `--faction-wow-on-fill`, free of charge for as long as the spine hue was itself a gold. White on those golds is 2.47:1 and 1.83:1. One ink cannot serve two grounds of opposite polarity (§"Contrast is a pairing"), so `--faction-wow-on-gold` carries the near-black the golds always had and nothing repaints. **Before changing a hue, grep its `-on-*` tokens for readers that are not the hue** — the borrow is invisible at the call site, which names a token and not a colour.

**And the rainbow does NOT follow a spine swap — but the ORDER does.** `--faction-default-stop-1…7` is a separately tuned spectrum — only three of seven were ever byte-identical to a faction hue — so the na yellow (#ca8a04) and teal (#1d6e72) stayed exactly where they were, and whether the wheel keeps a teal stop now that no faction owns teal is still an open owner call. `FACTION_RAINBOW_ORDER` is the other half of that sentence and went the other way: it sorts by hue, so #2068 left it wrong by two slots, and #2078 re-sorted it on the owner's ruling that the repaint of every stripe bar, pennant and legend is intended. It now runs **red → orange → gold → green → blue → plum → pink** (everymen, ua, ephemerists, snide, singularity, wow, coven), with the wheel cut at red because #c1272d sits at h357.7.

**Re-sorting is a legibility change, so it is measured, not just re-ordered.** The order exists so touching fills do not read as one colour, and the pair the swap put at risk is UA's sienna (h18) beside the Ephemerists' brass (h41) — the exact "goldenrod reads as a second brown" adjacency the old WOW yellow was tuned deep to avoid. It clears: ΔE2000 **31.1** light / **30.3** dark, against 34.2 / 31.0 for the yellow it replaces, and against **12.4 / 13.5** for everymen|ua, the tightest pair the app already ships. No new adjacency is tighter than that shipped floor; the tightest new one is wow|coven at 27.5 light / 16.9 dark. Separately, the brass and WOW's chronicle gold (#c8a02a) are ΔE **3.9** apart — effectively one colour — which is safe only because they never touch: WOW's rainbow stop is its plum, the chronicle gold is never a fill, and the two slugs sit at indices 2 and 5.

WOW's **skin is the chronicle**: cream parchment (`#fbf4e0`), a gold frame (`#c8a02a`) and plum ink (`#7a4a9e` light → `#c79be0` dark), MedievalSharp for display, the `✦` glyph, the googly-balloon verdict and an archaic register. None of that is yellow, and none of it should be pulled toward yellow.

The two axes are independent, and #830 collapsed them: it read a mislabelled mockup heading, gave the chronicle to Cozy Coven, and then *derived a whole yellow skin for WOW from its spine hue*, out of nothing. #838 retired that ramp. **ADR-0050** is the record — every design artifact for this faction pair is labelled backwards, so go by tokens and metaphor:

| | **`wow`** (Warriors of Whimsy) | **`coven`** (Cozy Coven) |
|---|---|---|
| Card | cream / gold / **plum** chronicle | **pink** marker sticker |
| Widget | googly **balloons** | **moon phases** on a night plate |
| Glyph | `✦` | `✨` |
| Tier ladder | `a start · quite solid · jolly good · splendid! · legendary!` | `sweet · lovely · wonderful · magical · iconic` |
| Register | archaic — *"Cast thy Verdict"* | cozy-casual — *"how'd this land?"* |

**On the hue.** Yellow is the palette's hard case (#651, #669, #677), for two reasons that pull in opposite directions. A yellow saturated enough to read as yellow needs **dark ink** on it, never white — hence `--faction-wow-on-fill` is ink in both themes. And WOW sits **adjacent to UA** in the spectrum, so the two are painted touching in the Leaderboard/DefaultPlayers stripe bars and Meadow's bloom; a goldenrod too near UA's burnt orange reads as a second brown at that size. The shipped light value resolves this by pushing hue rather than lightness — clear of orange, deep enough to stay legible.

**On the gold.** `#c8a02a` is **theme-invariant** — it is the same value in light and dark, because a metal-leaf frame does not brighten when the lights go out. The plum is what carries the theme flip. The gold is a *frame and rule* colour, never an ink: it measures 2.24:1 on the cream, so nothing legible is ever painted in it.

That constraint used to cost the design a deviation: #838's chronicle put the masthead **on** the gold/plum band, so both gradient stops had to be deepened until cream cleared 4.5:1 across it. #840 removed the cause rather than the symptom — the design's running head is a 6px stripe carrying **no text**, and the masthead is an eyebrow line inside the text column. With nothing painted on the band, the undimmed gold/plum stripe ships as drawn. **A contrast fix that fights the design is usually a structure bug**: check what put text on the surface before you dim the surface.

**On the mark — and the motto it no longer carries.** WOW's mark is `components/sigil/WowSigil.tsx`, and since **Sigil Studies v2** it is **the googly crown**: a three-peaked plum crown with two googly eyes rolling in it, one path, `fill-rule: evenodd`, nothing stroked, nothing layered and **nothing lettered**. It replaced #897's gilt coin — a beaded rope ring, a Pig-Latin motto band on a circular textPath reading **"IMSYWHAY · ORFAY · EVERYONEWAY"** (*whimsy for everyone*), and a cream field where a goofy unicorn brandished a floppy **noodle sword**. That coin was magnificent at 132px and unreadable below about 40; its own `MOTTO_FLOOR_PX = 56` — below which the band dropped its lettering and kept only its gold ring — was already an admission that a third of the mark had to go before the badge sizes, and at the 15px cell the design calls "the size that decides it" the whole thing antialiased into a gold smudge. **The Pig Latin and the noodle are still the faction's entire conceit**, and a version of WOW that "cleans them up" has drawn a different faction — but the crown spends that conceit on silhouette rather than on detail, so the words had to find another home. **They live in the copy now, not on the mark**: the Pig Latin is `feed:factionSelect.wow.tagline` and the hero motto tied to it, and the noodle sword is written into the letter (`wow.invitation.headline`, and its belonging perk since #2774). **When a mark stops carrying lettering, the words it carried do not become optional — find their slot in the catalog or they leave `src/` entirely**, which is exactly what happened here between #897's retirement and #2777. The mark stays **theme-invariant** either way, on the same reasoning as the gold above: a struck mark does not repaint itself when the lights go out, and the chrome that *mounts* it — the avatar's field disc — is what flips.

**On the lists.** WOW's duel surfaces (#895) are a tourney joust: a gold-framed enclosure, the gold/plum checker barrier along its top edge, and a **ribbon** that rides home with the loser — the loss floor dressed as generosity rather than as punishment. One rule in that skin is load-bearing and is not a taste call. The opponent's faction colour arrives on the rail and the seal (`accent`/`soft`, #310) and **can be any hue in the palette**, so it is held as a **rosette ring, a plate edge and a bar — never as an ink and never behind text**. That is what lets a hostile hue sit inside cream-and-gold chrome without a contrast fix, and it is why no `ARCHETYPE_PAIRS` row measures the opponent accent: there is no text pair to measure. This rule now lives on the **seal** skins (`components/duel/wowLists.tsx`); the duel *rail* was retired with the praxis-detail redesign (#1090), and `wowDuelRail.test.tsx` — which asserted the rule structurally against three different accents — went with it. It is guarded again: `components/duel/__tests__/wowSealAccent.test.tsx` (#1115) re-aims that assertion at the surface the rule now governs, walking both seal frames against those same three real accents in both modes. It stays **structural** because a *pairing* is invisible to a token-value sweep — `factionContrast.test.ts` knows which token holds which value and never which token was painted behind which string, and `duelSkinSlots.test.tsx` renders the seal against a foreign-faction opponent but only asserts slot presence. Both would stay green straight through the violation, which is the whole reason this guard is written by hand.

And the exposure widened before it narrowed. `DuelCard`'s ink seam (#1153) gives every praxis-detail skin three `color:` slots and a fill sitting directly behind a duellist's disc, on the one card where a rival's hue is nearest to hand — so the rule met a new, easy way to break it. **That seam is still unguarded**, and #1115 did not reach it: the violation there happens in the *call site* — a praxis-detail archetype choosing what to hand `ink` — so guarding it means walking the archetype registry, not the card. The mitigation is a comment on the prop and nothing stronger, which is worth stating plainly rather than dressing up: **where a rule has no guard, the seam that could violate it has to carry the rule in its own docstring**, at the point of use, not three files away in this document.

**On the phone.** The kit drew **one** mobile screen, not a mobile twin of every surface — a crested header wash, a list of gold-framed quest cards, and a bottom nav. #901 builds what is drawn (the field desk and the mobile task card) and **derives** the other four from that screen's chrome plus the matching desktop archetype, which is what every other faction's mobile build did. The vocabulary is one module, `components/factionMarks/wowMobile.tsx`, in the same shape as the duel skins' `components/duel/wowLists.tsx`.

Two of the drawing's pieces are deliberately **not** built, and the reason is the same one in both cases: they are not surfaces of ours. The phone bezel and the 9:41 clock are the *mockup's* device shell, and the app already runs inside a real one. The bottom nav is the global `MobileTabBar` — navigation means the same thing to every player, exactly as a level gem or a badge does (§6), so it must not acquire a faction seam. The header's `✦ 4,180` looks like part of that shell and is not: it is the player's score, and it survives.

The kit's mobile palette is a complete two-theme contract, and all but two of its keys resolved to tokens WOW already shipped — the mapping lives beside the declarations in `index.css`. One pairing failed measurement: the kit sets the header byline in its `sub` grey, which reads 4.10:1 on the header's lower gradient stop, so that one line takes `--faction-wow-accent-deep` instead. That is the third slice in this epic to find the kit's contrast claims wrong; **measure every pairing you lift from a design annotation.**

Read this next to the Albescent note above: those are the repo's two partly-registered factions, partial on **different axes**. Albescent has neither theme nor skin, on purpose, because it is hiding. WOW now has both, and most of the manifest claims it — the cards and their stamps, the vote widget, the composer, the crest and avatar, the comment and feed frame, the page-level hero/backdrop/profile/pledge surfaces, the duel seal, three mobile surfaces, and both **detail pages**: the task detail from #1037 and the praxis detail from #1121. **Nothing still renders `Default*`: WOW claims all twenty surfaces** (#2534). This sentence used to point at `factions/__tests__/wowRendersDefault.test.tsx` for the list of unclaimed ones "with the reason each one is unclaimed" — but the list had emptied, and that file kept its answer in a hand-maintained set, so a NEW surface key defaulted to unclaimed and it would have asserted WOW falls back on it. It is gone; `factions/__tests__/surfaceDispatch.test.ts` derives the same claim from the registry and raises its own bar when a core surface lands. A number cached here goes stale every time a surface ships or is retired, and this sentence drifted three times before it stopped naming one — so it names no count now, only where the count is computed.

**Its task card and its praxis card do not match, on purpose.** A quest is *issued* by DECREE and proof is *recorded* in the CHRONICLE: `WowTaskCard` is a sheet hung from a knobbed rod under a gold/plum checker band, sealed with the crest; `WowPraxisCard` is a bound chronicle with a running head and a score stamp. They share the palette, the two fonts and the ✦, and nothing else. #785's "the praxis card mirrors the task card" clause is retired for this faction (#899) — the mismatch is the archetype, not a bug to reconcile.

### A retired metaphor's last surface is a GROUND, and it is the one nobody lists (#1208, #1209)

Two factions finished the same migration a day apart, and both issues were filed as a list of components. Coven's `coven.exe` window and the Ephemerists' illuminated codex had each been superseded by a v2 card metaphor, and each left its old token family painting a dozen surfaces the redesign never reached. Sweeping those is bookkeeping. Four things about it are not.

**The page under the surfaces is one of the surfaces.** `.eph-backdrop` is a `position: fixed` ground rendered by `FactionBackdrop` behind exactly two routes — the faction page and a member's profile — and both were on the sweep's list while the ground under them was not, because a CSS class is not a component and nothing greps for it. Repainting the plates and leaving the ground would have produced the failure in its most visible form: papyrus floating on vellum, full-bleed. **When a token family retires, grep the stylesheet for its class rules, not just the components for its `var()` reads** — the two censuses do not overlap, and the stylesheet's half is the bigger surface. Four dead animation classes fell out of the same look, along with the one keyframe that had to *stay* because a different faction's dust motes had adopted it.

**The guard is a source scan, and it is worth more than any per-file render test.** One assertion that no `.ts`/`.tsx` under `src` contains the retired prefix covers every surface, present and future, including the ones inside a `membership.state === 'gate'` branch or an empty state that no fixture reaches. Strip comments before matching: a docstring explaining what a file is *not* painting is the commonest false positive, and both sweeps' guards would have failed on their own headers otherwise.

**Do not delete the family, and say why at its declaration.** `--eph-*` survived the sweep with zero component readers because the `--faction-ephemerists-card-*` contract every faction supplies (§3) *aliased six of its members*, and those are read through `factionCssVar` by surfaces that are not Ephemerists skins at all. A count of zero `var(--eph-` reads was therefore not a licence to delete — the family was reached by indirection only, which is exactly the state that looks dead to a grep.

**And an indirection is a stay of execution, not a pardon (#1627).** Those six aliases were the last thing holding the codex up, and four of them were colours: the card contract has moved onto the Valley plate, and what is left of `--eph-*` is two FONT aliases. The colour half has no reader by any route now, and the `ephemerists vellum …` rows in `factionContrast.test.ts` are measuring a palette nothing paints — including one that is still spending a #651 BASELINE entry on a 3.72:1 vermilion no surface prints. The rule the paragraph above gives still holds and is worth restating from the other end: **when the last indirection goes, the family and the rows that measured it retire together, in one change.** A test that keeps measuring a dead palette is a test that has stopped testing, and it will read as coverage in every future audit.

**And a colour's role can be decided by a measurement rather than by the design.** The Valley plate already knew `-brass` is a rule colour and never an ink (2.97:1 on papyrus). The sweep found the second one: `-plate-ochre` measured **4.46:1 on the plate and 4.18 on the page** — a near miss the task card never hit, because it only ever drew the fifth tally stroke in it. The design set the roster's "Rival" chip in ochre, and that was the one thing in the drawing that could not ship as drawn: ochre is a mark, a rule and a fill on this skin, and the accent *ink* is `-nile`. Those readings are the papyrus half's, and #1627 retired the papyrus (below); what survives the repaint is the ruling, which is what a role is. **`-brass` clears 7.22:1 on the night plate and is still not an ink** — one token, one role, decided once and not re-litigated every time the ground moves under it.

A moved ground also moves the rows that measured it. The duel seal's ledger band went from the codex's vellum-deep to the plate's panel cell, and the two `factionContrast` rows pointing at it were repointed and re-measured rather than left to keep passing against a sheet no surface renders. That is the §3 rule about re-measuring a surface that gained a sheet, running in the other direction.

**And the harder case is a family that FORKS rather than retires, because no retirement grep can find it (#2039).** Everything above assumes the old family dies, which is what makes it findable: one source scan for the dead prefix covers every surface. Singularity's did not. Task cards v3 grounded its chassis on `--faction-singularity-term-*`, while `--faction-singularity-card-*` stayed fully alive for the avatar, the praxis card, the seal, the hero and the profile frame — so `SingularityBackdrop`, still painting `-card-bg` plus `-border-hard`, kept reading tokens that were current, live and simply **no longer the ones the card standing on it used**. Nothing was retired, so nothing was greppable.

The damage that hid behind that is the theme half, and it is worth stating as a rule of its own: **a ground and the surface mounted on it must have the same light/dark behaviour, or the page freezes while the card moves.** `-card-*` is theme-invariant, `-term-*` restates seventeen of nineteen members under dark. So the **light** page was painted in the DARK chassis's own colour, and the **dark** page in the dark chassis's — ΔE **0.0** from the card standing on it, no fill separation at all. Both halves broke, in opposite directions, from one frozen token.

Three things generalise from the fix.

- **The census that finds this is per-faction, not per-prefix**: for each `backdrop` surface, list the tokens it paints and the tokens its card grounds on, and ask whether the two sets flip together. Run against all seven, the other six were already consistent — Coven, Everymen, UA and WOW flip on both sides; Ephemerists is invariant on both (its register declares nothing under dark, #1627); S.N.I.D.E. flips its wall and its clipping stock together while `-acid` / `-pink` are invariant on the page and on the card alike.
- **A page ground is worth its own token even when the value already exists elsewhere.** `--faction-singularity-term-page` (named after `--faction-ephemerists-plate-page`) reuses two blacks the palette already had — the light desk is the dark chassis, the dark desk is the score stamp's well — so the diff mints a *name*, not paint. That is §8's test answered: a different fill is a variable's job.
- **Do not fix a ΔE 0.0 that a gradient and a frame already answer.** WOW and Everymen read 0.0 too, at the top of the viewport, because each backdrop's final layer is an opaque gradient starting at the card's own stock (`--faction-wow-ground-from` *is* `--faction-wow-card-bg`; `.em-backdrop` starts at `--everymen-paper`) and running to a deep twin. Both cards carry a printed frame, both grounds carry a vignette, and both tokens are load-bearing for measured inks elsewhere — `--faction-wow-ground-from` in particular was chosen against a 4.52:1 secondary reading under a frosted card. **Repointing those would re-open a measured argument to buy a separation the frame already draws**, which is the "somewhere worse" a palette alignment is supposed to avoid.

### A faction that goes dark in light mode goes dark all at once (#1627, #1636)

> **SUPERSEDED for the Ephemerists by #2141** — see "A card register belongs to the ground its CONSUMERS paint" at the end of this section. The *diagnosis* below (one set of card ink names cannot serve two grounds of opposite polarity) is sound and still the thing to look for; the *resolution* was backwards. The register is no longer theme-invariant, the plate is vellum by day, and the Ephemerists are not a dark-in-light card faction. Read what follows as the finding, not as the current state.

The card contract was the codex's last redoubt, and moving it was filed as four lines. It is not four lines, and the reason is worth keeping because it generalises to every faction: **`--faction-{key}-card-*` and a skin's own private ground are not independent.** `praxisCard/shared.tsx::sheetInk()` reads one set of card ink names for a card that grounds on `-plate-bg`, so pointing `-card-bg` at the cornice band made Ephemerists dark-in-light while its praxis sheet was still papyrus — and the luminance intervals that clear 4.5:1 on the two grounds **do not intersect** (>= 0.2497 on the band, <= 0.1212 on the papyrus). No value satisfies both. Applying the four lines alone takes the contrast guard from 691 green to five failures, and every escape from that is worse than the disease: sibling `-plate-notice` tokens are eight factions × three to do properly, and a slug branch in `sheetInk` is the shape #1359 exists to remove.

So **the palette moves as one unit**, and the Valley plate is theme-INVARIANT at its night values. Light mode adopts the design's own dark drawing rather than a fresh tuning: a light band spliced onto a dark sheet is a palette nobody drew, and the night half was measured as a whole.

**What the peers share with it is polarity, not one palette — and this is the repo's first FACTION REGISTER whose dark cascade declares nothing.** Being exact matters here, because the loose version of the sentence ("the way Singularity and S.N.I.D.E. already are") is simply false, and it would license the next faction to skip a measurement on the strength of a pattern that does not exist. S.N.I.D.E. and Singularity are the other two skins that are dark in *both* cascades (§6), and both are still **tuned per theme**: Singularity restates seventeen of its nineteen `--faction-singularity-term-*` tokens under `[data-theme="dark"]` with different values (only `-cta-bg` and `-cta-ink` hold) (`-bg` #07130c against #050f08, `-ink` #22c55e against #4ade80, `-blue` #4c7fef against #60a5fa), and S.N.I.D.E. is a real precedent for its `-ink`/`-paper` pair while its `-wall-*` family flips outright. Ephemerists is the first *register* to declare nothing under dark — a claim about *this* palette, drawn for the night and measured on both app grounds, not a shape to copy on sight. Note the qualifier: several ORNAMENT palettes already declare nothing under dark (`--faction-coven-moon-*`, `--faction-wow-crest-*`, `--faction-snide-amp-*`, `--faction-singularity-vote-*`), but an ornament is a handful of marks on a ground somebody else measured. A register IS the ground. What still flips for the faction is its spine hue's fill, wash and border, which are not the skin.

Three things fall out that are the point rather than side effects:

- **The plate's ink budget stops being a budget.** On papyrus, which ink was allowed on which of four grounds was a real question with three near-misses in it. Every plate ink now clears 4.5:1 on every plate ground, the tightest being `-ochre` at 4.99 in a panel cell. What that does *not* buy is a licence to paint anything anywhere: `-brass` and `-ochre` are rules and marks by role, and the roles outlived the measurement that first drew them.
- **Three token pairs become synonyms** — `-muted`/`-quiet`, `-caption`/`-brass`, `-gold`/`-band-ink` are each one value under two names now, because the walk-downs that separated them existed only on the papyrus. They were already identical in the dark half, so this is not new; it is newly *total*, and it is a name pretending to be a value (#1213) waiting for its follow-up.
- **The functional inks flip with polarity, not with theme.** Ephemerists' `-card-notice` / `-alarm` / `-credit` join S.N.I.D.E.'s and Singularity's bright values in the LIGHT cascade. A faction going dark-in-light owes that block a visit; nothing else notices, because each of those inks is separately AA-clear on the sheet it used to sit on.

**And a ground the whole faction shares is the last thing to be measured.** Two live failures fell out of a repaint that had nothing to do with either. The player-profile header sets its eyebrow in the kit's `muted` — `-plate-quiet`, an ink measured on the plate, the page and the panel cells — and mounts it on the cornice **band**, where it read **2.07:1** and had since the skin shipped. `VoteShell`'s logged-out gate paints `-card-accent` and `EphemeristsVote` returns it *before* drawing its own vote plate, so the ink lands on `-plate-bg`: **3.75:1** in dark. Neither pairing was in the manifest, and neither could have been — the manifest is authored from the roles index.css states in words, and "a kit hands `muted` to a header whose ground is a different token" is not one of them. **When a kit assembles an (ink, ground) pair out of two separately-measured tokens, that pair is a manifest row.** Both are rows now.

One drawing decision rode along and is worth recording because it is not a colour. The codex struck a person in a list as a **circular** vellum medallion, on five surfaces. The plate's whole geometry is the cut corner, and the design draws every roster row, comment row and byline with an **octagon-clipped monogram** — so `AuthorOctagon` is now the kit's single answer to "draw a player", and the Ephemerists show a circle nowhere except the shared `FactionAvatar`, whose shape belongs to the app rather than to the faction.

**The cut corner answers "draw a player", not "draw everything" (#2037).** The sentence above is easy to read one clause too wide, so pin the edge: task cards v3 strikes the Ephemerists' points on a **compass rose** — a ruled disc with four needles on the cardinals — where the v2 card cut a stepped octagon. That is not a relapse. `AuthorOctagon` is still the kit's single answer to a *person*, and four other plate surfaces still cut the octagon; what changed is that the points plate was never a cartouche. It is an **instrument** on a field journal, and the shape a surveyor's plate reaches for is round. The test is which question the shape answers: a cartouche frames somebody's name, so it takes the plate's geometry; an instrument is drawn the way the instrument is drawn. The mark lives in `factionMarks/ephemeristsPlate.tsx` with a `size` prop rather than in the card, because the praxis card's score stamp strikes the identical medallion and the owner has ruled the two unify (#2042) — a mark on two surfaces is drawn once, or it drifts the first time one of them is redrawn.

**When a word turns, the ANIMATION is the clock and the word underneath never moves (#2038).** Task cards v3 turns the Ephemerists' points label and sign-up through five scripts — the live catalogue string, then `PVNCTA`, then the same word in Arabic, Japanese and cuneiform. Three rules make that shippable rather than a pile of accessibility debt, and all three generalise to any rotating ornament. **Frame 1 is the i18n string, not a hardcoded English word**, so a Spanish site turns `Puntos` while the ornament frames stay fixed; the non-Latin frames are constants in the component and never catalogue entries, because in `feed.json` they would be four strings no translator can act on (#440). **The whole stack is `aria-hidden` over one visually-hidden copy of that string**, so the accessible name is the word at every instant — never a glyph, never a concatenation of the two. And **the frame is advanced by `animationiteration` on a class gated on `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`**, which is what makes the reduced-motion behaviour free: no animation, no iteration, no advance, and the label rests on frame 1 with nothing in the component branching on the preference. A `setInterval` in a `useEffect` would have needed a JS twin of that media query and a second, divergent definition of "stilled".

**And a rotation owes WCAG 2.2.2 a mechanism, which `aria-hidden` does not supply (#2038).** Auto-updating content that starts by itself and sits beside other content must be pausable, stoppable or hideable — and "it is decoration hidden from assistive tech" answers the screen-reader question while leaving the sighted reader with an unstoppable turn. Two mechanisms ship. The platform's reduce-motion setting stills it outright, which WCAG's own glossary admits: a mechanism "may be relied upon to be provided by either the platform or by user agents". And a `:hover` / `:focus-within` rule on the card root sets `animation-play-state: paused` on both turns — a pause proper, not a reset, so the frame showing stays showing. That second one earns its keep twice over: it is also why the CTA's word cannot change under a cursor or a focus ring on its way to a click. **The residual gap is a touch reader whose OS carries no reduce-motion setting**, who has neither; closing it needs a site-wide motion preference, which is a decision above any one card.

**Pin a turning label's box by LAYOUT, never by measuring it (#2038).** The design measures every variant and pins a pixel width, so the button cannot resize under the word. Rendering all five variants into one CSS grid cell (`grid-area: 1 / 1`, all but the current one `visibility: hidden`) buys the same box for nothing: the cell is as wide as the widest script by definition, and it cannot go stale when a webfont swaps in and changes a variant's width — which a measurement taken at mount would. It also survives this repo's test harness, where effects never run and `getBoundingClientRect` returns zeroes.

**A monogram slot is never empty (#1664).** A display name can be nothing but whitespace — creation strips and rejects a blank one, but `CharacterUpdate.display_name` carries only a `max_length`, so a `PATCH` lands `"   "` in the column — and a drawn disc with no glyph in it reads as a render that failed rather than as a player with no name. The Ephemerists kit's `initialsOf` returns **`"·"`** in that case, and the plate's surfaces take the kit's helper rather than each carrying their own; the task page was the one that had drifted, and it drifted on exactly that input. The other kits answer for themselves (the Coven's mark is the same; the faction pages' single-letter `initial` falls back to `"?"`) — what does not vary is that *something* is struck.

### Verify a moved `index.css` block in the BUILT stylesheet, not by counting braces

`index.css` is now large enough that skin work routinely moves whole token blocks between the light declarations, the `[data-theme="dark"]` cascade and the media queries. **A dropped `}` fails nothing.** With CSS nesting and `@media` blocks, a lost closing brace produces no parse error — it silently **reparents** everything after it, and the result is valid CSS that simply applies to almost nobody. This wave a whole token block ended up nested inside `@media (prefers-reduced-motion)`; `tsc`, `eslint` and `vitest` were green throughout, and only `vite build` plus reading the emitted file caught it.

Balanced brace counts prove nothing here — the file balances either way, the braces just close different things than you meant. After moving or merging a block, run `vite build` and grep the **emitted** stylesheet for the moved selectors, checking the **depth** each one sits at. The question is never "is the selector present", it is "is it still at the nesting level it was written for".

---

## 4. Typography

All eighteen families come from the Google Fonts catalogue and are **self-hosted** (#1977): `frontend/scripts/fetch-fonts.mjs` downloads the woff2 cuts into `src/assets/fonts/` and generates **two** stylesheets. There is no request to `fonts.googleapis.com` any more.

Two consequences for style work. **Adding a family is now a repo change, not a URL edit** — put it in `FACES` in that script and re-run it; hand-editing a generated stylesheet is how a `src:` path drifts from the file on disk, which fails silently to a generic serif. And **`font-display: swap` is carried on every face**, unchanged from the old `&display=swap`, so text paints in the fallback and swaps in — the flash is deliberate, not a bug to tune away.

### Three of the eighteen families block first paint, and which three is a REACHABILITY fact (#2079)

`src/fonts.css` is `@import`ed by `index.css`, so every `@font-face` rule in it lands in the one render-blocking stylesheet. 62 of the 82 rules were for the fifteen families **only a faction surface can render in** — 19% of that sheet, on a budget that had 774 bytes of headroom under its FAIL ceiling. They now live in `src/fonts.faction.css`, which nothing `@import`s: `src/factionFaces.ts` is its only importer, it is reached across a chunk boundary, and Vite therefore emits it as a second CSS asset that `dist/index.html` never mentions. Measured 24,226 → 23,122 gzipped; the raw bytes are unchanged, split across two files, and no woff2 moved.

`SHELL_FAMILIES` in `fetch-fonts.mjs` is where the line is drawn, and it holds Lora, Courier Prime and Bebas Neue — the `--font-display` / `--font-body` / `--font-accent` triple, which is also Tailwind's three family utilities and therefore reachable from the nav, the sidebar and every neutral page.

Three things worth carrying.

**The question is not "is this a faction's face", it is "can a chunk that draws it fetch the sheet".** Bebas Neue *is* a faction face — it is `--font-faction-poster` and S.N.I.D.E.'s condensed cut — and it stays in the blocking sheet anyway, because the na card font and the accent utility reach it from the shell. Conversely the rule that decides where a family goes has nothing to do with how many factions use it: Cormorant Garamond is drawn by four factions and by the Albescent letter, and all five are behind a chunk boundary.

**"Some archetype imports this component" is the wrong test, and it passes on a real defect.** The natural read of §1.6 — faction identity cascades from the card archetype — suggests the archetype loader is the only seam that needs to ask for the sheet, and `lazyArchetype`'s `preload()` does cover ~150 of them. But a module can be loaded by a chunk that has no archetype in it: `DefaultProfileBody` is imported both by `AlbescentProfileBody` (an archetype) and directly by `FactionProfileBody`, so an **unaffiliated** player's profile draws the duel-victor badge's Cinzel ring legend with nothing having asked. The unit of the question is a **load root** — `main.tsx` and every `import()` target — and "can everything in *its* static graph get the sheet". Four modules failed that and now import `factionFaces` themselves; `VoteShell` is the sharpest, since its logged-out gate paints six faction voices for a viewer who has no faction at all.

**Both ways of getting this wrong are invisible, so the guard reads the module graph rather than a screenshot.** Strand a family and `font-display: swap` paints the fallback and leaves it there — #839's failure class, where the fallback *is* the rendering. Wire the sheet back onto the critical path with one static import from anything eager and the build stays green while the whole win evaporates, the only symptom a number in `bundle-budget.mjs`. `factionFaceSplit.test.ts` asserts both directions; `fontsLoaded.test.ts`'s three questions are unchanged and now read the two sheets as one loader, because "is it loaded at all" and "which sheet is it in" are different questions.

The weight subsetting the two guards below enforce is unchanged by self-hosting: exactly 41 faces ship, which is exactly the set the stylesheets declare.

| Role           | Font            | Usage                                     |
| -------------- | --------------- | ----------------------------------------- |
| Display / Logo | `Lora` (italic) | Wordmark, page titles, praxis titles      |
| Body / UI      | `Courier Prime` | All body text, labels, nav links, filters |
| Accent display | `Bebas Neue`    | Reserved for special uses                 |

**Per-faction headline fonts** — each faction card uses its own display font for the headline/title, exposed via `--faction-{slug}-card-font`:

| Faction     | Headline font      | CSS var                           |
| ----------- | ------------------ | --------------------------------- |
| UA          | `Cormorant Garamond` — via `--font-faction-serif`; `EB Garamond` is its secondary, on `--faction-ua-body-font` (#848) | `--faction-ua-card-font`          |
| Everymen    | `Special Elite`    | `--faction-everymen-card-font`    |
| Cozy Coven  | `Caveat`           | `--faction-coven-card-font`       |
| Warriors of Whimsy | `MedievalSharp` — the chronicle's display face; `Lora` italic is its secondary (§3) | `--faction-wow-card-font` |
| S.N.I.D.E.  | `Permanent Marker` (+ a punk set: Anton / Bebas Neue / Archivo Black / Special Elite, via `--faction-snide-font-*`) | `--faction-snide-card-font`       |
| Ephemerists | `Cinzel`           | `--faction-ephemerists-card-font` |
| Singularity | `Share Tech Mono`  | `--faction-singularity-card-font` |

UA Masters had a row here for a font it could not render in: it is dormant until Era 2 (§6), `--faction-ua-masters-card-font` was never declared, and #1293 took UnifrakturCook off the loader as an orphan. Its intended face is recorded in §6 with the rest of the archetype, which is where a faction with no component belongs. **A table of live tokens is not the place to hold a plan.**

Use `factionCssVar(slug, 'card-font')` in components. Never hardcode the font family string directly.

**A face can belong to a SURFACE rather than to a faction.** `--faction-{slug}-card-font` is read by a dozen surfaces each, so repointing one to satisfy a single redesign restyles eleven others by accident. When a design names a face for one surface only, give it a shared `--font-faction-*` token and reference that token from the one component — the same move `DefaultTaskCard` makes when it takes Lora rather than `--faction-default-card-font`. The v2 task cards (#1023) introduced four such faces: **Quicksand** (`--font-faction-rounded`) and **Grenze Gotisch** (`--font-faction-witch`) for Coven's spell slip, **Poiret One** (`--font-faction-deco`) and **Spectral** (`--font-faction-spectral`) for the Ephemerists plate. Coven's `card-font` is still Caveat and Ephemerists' is still Cinzel, and both still appear on their v2 cards — as the hand-lettering and the small caps respectively.

Whichever route a face takes, **it must also be in the `index.html` loader**, added in the same commit. A family named but never requested renders as its generic fallback, and that fallback *is* the rendering — no check catches it except `fontsLoaded` comparing the two lists (#839).

**And the token must be read, or it is the family's only consumer (#1293).** The reverse comparison asks "does anything name this family", which the guard answered by searching source — with `index.css` in the corpus, so a token's own declaration satisfied it and **every `--font-*` token vouched for itself**. Five had no `var()` reader at all and the check had been green over them for months; UnifrakturCook was on the render-blocking third-party request with nothing behind it but the line that named it. The question the guard asks now is whether anything *reads* `var(--font-…)`, and an unread declaration is cut out of the corpus before the family search runs. Two things this pattern teaches. **A declaration is not a use** — the same shape as §3's dead `--eph-*` family, in reverse: there the family was alive by indirection and looked dead to a grep, here the token was dead and looked alive to one. And **an unread token does not always free its family**: `--font-faction-old` had no reader, but `MediaArt`'s before/after plates set `IM Fell English` as a literal, so the token went and the face stayed. Deleting the family on the strength of the token alone would have unstyled a live surface — check the reads before the request.

**A face may be subset to a LITERAL STRING, and then it must never be inlined (#2038).** The kit's eighteen families keep Google's own per-script cuts, so an English page fetches only `latin`. That does not scale to a script the site prints four characters of: Noto Serif JP alone is ~120 cuts and megabytes. For those, `scripts/fetch-fonts.mjs` has a second list, `TEXT_FACES`, which requests the v1 API's `text=` parameter and gets back **one** `@font-face` over a font containing exactly the codepoints asked for — ten codepoints across three Noto faces, 1.8 to 4.4 KB each, for +247 gzipped bytes of critical CSS. Two traps ride along. **`text` is a contract with the component**, not a tuning: a character dropped from it renders as tofu, and the per-OS fallbacks (`Geeza Pro` macOS, `Segoe UI Historic` Windows) mean the reviewer on the wrong machine sees nothing wrong — so a test reads the frames out of the component and checks each codepoint against the shipped `unicode-range`. And **a font that small trips Vite's 4 KB inline limit**: two of the three went straight into the render-blocking stylesheet as base64 and put the CSS budget 5.5 KB over its FAIL ceiling in one build. `vite.config.ts` now refuses to inline any `.woff2` at all, which is the right rule anyway — a data: URI is fetched by every reader before first paint, which is precisely the `unicode-range` laziness self-hosting was for.

**And the loaded family is not the loaded FACE (#1294).** One axis further down, the same failure changes shape: a weight a stylesheet sets and the request omits does not fall back to a generic, it *resolves* — the browser picks the nearest requested face and paints. Sixty `--font-display` sites set no weight, so they asked for 400 and rendered in Lora **500**; twenty more asked for bold and rendered **semibold**; the duel-victor badge's `DUEL`/`VICTOR` legend has declared Cinzel 800 and rendered 700 since #1304 dropped that axis. Nothing looks broken in any of them, which is why it survives review. **Owner ruling: the stylesheet is the truth and the loader follows it** — where a surface declares a weight, request that weight, rather than rewriting the declaration to name whichever face the browser happened to substitute. If the real face then differs from the design sheet, the sheet is what gets synced.

Three things that ruling turns up. **Adding a face can retire one**: Lora upright 500 was on the request only because those sixty 400-sites resolved to it, so requesting the 400 left it with no reader and it came off in the same commit — the two directions of the guard are one system, not two lists. **Where the family has no such face, the declaration is the thing that is wrong**: Bebas Neue ships exactly one face, upright 400, and Google Fonts has no bold of it, so `fontWeight: 700` on the na heading was buying a *synthesised* fake-bold over an already-condensed display face; the weight goes, and that is the honest move rather than the cheap one. And **a family whose name is a weight is still a family** — `Archivo Black` is served at `font-weight: 400`, so `--faction-snide-font-black` picks a cut, not a weight, and the dozen surfaces reading it were never asking for 900. Slant is the one axis still unguarded: the same Bebas Neue takes `fontStyle: italic` on five `na` surfaces and every one of those obliques is synthetic, because telling "this family has no italic" from "this family has an italic we did not request" needs Google's catalogue rather than our source.

**Type scale** is defined as CSS variables in `index.css`. Use the variable names, not raw pixel values. It comes in **two tiers**, and the naming convention is the tier boundary made visible: the label tier is a size ramp with t-shirt names, the content tier is a **role vocabulary**.

| Tier        | Tokens                                                                     | Rule                    |
| ----------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------- |
| **Label**   | `--text-xs` 8 · `--text-sm` 9 · `--text-base` 10 · `--text-md` 11 · `--text-lg` 12 · `--text-xl` 14 | Stays small. Scanned, not read — **except the caption role**, below. |
| **Content** | `--text-content` 18 · `--text-title` 24 · `--text-heading` 32 · `--text-display` 42 | The floor and up. Read for meaning. |

The content tier is a clean **4:3 ramp** — each step is exactly a third bigger than the last, so it needs no table to reproduce.

| Token             | px  | Role                                                    |
| ----------------- | --- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
| `--text-content`  | 18  | body copy, descriptions, admin notes — **the floor**    |
| `--text-title`    | 24  | titles, scores                                          |
| `--text-heading`  | 32  | section and page headings                               |
| `--text-display`  | 42  | hero, wordmark                                          |

**Content-text floor.** `--text-content` (18px) is the floor for _real content_ — nothing a player is meant to actually read may sit below it. The name *is* the rule.

### The role vocabulary

Classify every string against these three roles. This is the vocabulary the sweeps work from.

- **Content → the floor.** User-authored free text (`praxis.body`, `task.description`, `admin_note`, comments). Titles (`h1`–`h3`, `font-display`, `task.title`). Numbers a player cares about (points, votes, level). Full sentences from the i18n catalog — banner prose, status explanations.
- **Label → stays small,** and splits in two (#1307). `.label-heading` for a region name; `.label-caption` for a small fact about the thing. `.eyebrow` was the retired form of both, and anything uppercase + letter-spacing hand-rolled inline is the same thing wearing no name. Button and link chrome. Pills, badges, stamps, corner counters. Bylines, timestamps, metadata.
- **Ornament → exempt.** Glyphs used as icons (a `✗` dingbat is not text). Text that is part of the illustration — stamp text, tape-strip labels, punch-card headers.

### The geometry doctrine

> **If the type doesn't fit, the container is too small. Make the container bigger.**
> Type wins; geometry yields. A cramped card is not a reason to shrink readable text —
> it is a reason to widen the card.

Both of the floor's original exceptions came from treating a fixed container as immovable. It is not. (The floor now carries **one standing exception**, `.label-caption` at 12px — stated below, because it is a tier rather than a site and the doctrine above cannot be acted on for a timestamp.)

**One container really is immovable: a drawn one (#1147).** When ornament type sits *inside a mark* — a
figure in UA's ensō, a total in Everymen's roundel — the container is an illustration, and widening it
is not a layout decision but a redrawing. There the numeral takes a **ceiling**: the `--text-*` tier is
the largest it may be, and the mark's own clear width caps it once the string grows. Two consequences
worth stating, because both have been got wrong: the ceiling must be a no-op at the values the site
actually shows today — a mark whose figure shrinks on a routine score has been mis-tuned, not fitted —
and a caption sharing the mark keeps its own full line box, so the figure can never come to rest on the
word. A cropped `line-height` under a figure is the bug, every time. This carve-out is for **marks
only**: a card, a panel or a rail is not a drawing, and there the doctrine above stands unchanged.

**Role classes.** Two classes carry the content tier, next to the label tier's own pair:

| Class            | Token                   | Role                                                                     |
| ---------------- | ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `.content-text`  | `--text-content` (18px) | body copy, descriptions, admin notes, the praxis body, textareas          |
| `.content-title` | `--text-title` (24px)   | titles and scores                                                        |

There is deliberately no `.content-heading` / `.content-display` / `.content-score`: each would have a single caller already owned by a component, and a class for one caller is a class for nobody. A score is a title-sized number — `.content-title` plus a `fontWeight`.

**Label text:** Courier Prime, off the `--text-*` label ramp, painted in `var(--label-ink)`. Use `.label-heading` or `.label-caption` — the split below says which. Never add an inline `fontSize`, `letterSpacing` or neutral `color` to an element that already carries one: the class owns all three, and restating the neutral is what stops a faction frame repointing the tier. **`.eyebrow` is gone** (#1307): 124 class sites across 61 files moved onto the pair, and the rule was deleted with the last of them.

That ink is the third text tier, and §3 records what it can and cannot do. It clears AA on every neutral stock it lands on — **5.40:1** on the page, 5.78 over the frost, 5.07 on the alt surface, 4.78 on the filter well in light; 6.21 / 5.67 / 5.37 / 5.47 in dark — and it is now visibly a different ink from `--color-text-secondary` rather than the same one under a second name. What it does **not** have is headroom: the tier sits at secondary's weight because the palette has no AA-clear room below secondary, so a label cannot be made quieter by walking its colour down. If a label rank needs to recede further, the levers left are size, tracking, casing and layout — not ink.

### A wordmark is a MARK, so it scales — it never breaks mid-word (#2000)

Four of the seven faction-page heroes set `overflow-wrap: anywhere` on the `<h1>` they render the
faction name into. On a phone the Everymen hero printed **"EVER / YMEN"**, and the name it showed was
not the name — which is why this is a different failure from a paragraph wrapping awkwardly. A faction
name is the **mark**, in the same family as the sigil beside it: the reader identifies the faction by
its shape, so a break that invents a new word is worse than an overflow, and `break-anywhere` is simply
the wrong rule to have on it. **No hero wordmark carries one.** `frontend/src/components/factionHero/__tests__/factionWordmarkWrap.test.tsx`
holds that across all seven.

The name surfaced on Everymen because "Everymen" is the longest **single-word** name — every other
hero's name is two glyphs (`UA`), carries a space to wrap at (`Cozy Coven`, `Warriors of Whimsy`,
`The Ephemerists`) or carries dots (`S.N.I.D.E.`). All four heroes that had the rule carried the latent
bug; only one had a name long enough to trip it. **A rule that a name's current spelling hides is still
a bug**, so it came off Coven, UA and WOW too rather than only where the screenshot was taken.

Deleting the rule is only half a fix, because the geometry doctrine above then applies and the container
has to give. Two levers, and the Everymen hero needed both:

- **The wordmark's own flex track needs a floor.** Its column carried `min-width: 0`, and its cog seal
  is an inline flex *sibling* — so on a 340px phone the seal plus the gap ate 148px of a ~222px row and
  the mark had ~75px to set 76px type in. `min-width: min(240px, 100%)` plus `flex-wrap: wrap` stands
  the seal *above* the name at that width, which is what Coven (250), UA (260) and the Ephemerists (220)
  already do. `min(…, 100%)` and not a bare `240`: a hard floor pushes the column past the hero's own
  clipped edge on a phone, which is #1314's lesson on the S.N.I.D.E. hero.
- **The mark caps rather than reflows.** `font-size: min(76px, 20vw)` — 76px is the poster size and the
  ceiling, and the `vw` arm only bites below a ~380px viewport, where even the *full* column cannot hold
  76px type. This is the drawn-container carve-out above read the other way round: a wordmark is not
  inside a mark, it **is** one, so it takes a ceiling at its poster size and scales under it instead of
  breaking. Above ~380px nothing moves, which is the test that the cap is fitted and not a redesign.

**A test that forbids a Tailwind utility must not spell it.** The check above originally wrote
`word-break: break-all` as a plain string. Tailwind scans `src` for class candidates — **test files and
comments included** — so the literal emitted that utility into the *blocking* stylesheet: +17 B gzip9,
which put the initial-load CSS budget exactly on its WARN line for the sake of a test fixture. The
value is now assembled with `join('-')` and appears nowhere bare, and the built sheet is byte-identical
to before the fix. The same trap applies to any assertion naming a class the code must *not* have.

**The ceiling is universal; the ARM is per-hero, and `vw` is only right when the track is a fraction of
the viewport (#2222).** "Singularity" is the second name long enough to trip the geometry — the only
other single-word one, eleven characters of Share Tech Mono, which advances **0.54em per glyph**, so with
its 0.04em tracking the mark is **6.38em** wide, or 357px at the hero's 56px display size. It has no space
and no dots, so nothing about the name gives, and the hero clips its own overflow (correctly — that
`overflow: hidden` holds the corner glow and the scanlines to the tile), so the failure printed as
*Singularit* plus a sheared descender rather than as a spill.

Everymen's `20vw` arm is not transferable to it. That hero's mark sits in a track that really is roughly
the viewport minus a seal; **Singularity's is not** — a fixed 240px readout column sits beside it inside
the hero, and the 320px desktop rail sits outside it, so one viewport width yields three different track
widths. An arm fitted to a phone caps out around 430px and is inert by 768px, which leaves the mark
overflowing in two bands a phone screenshot never shows: **~768–800px**, and **~1024–1160px with the rail
open**. So the arm reads the column instead: `container-type: inline-size` on the identity column and
`font-size: min(56px, 14cqw)` on the mark. 6.38 x 0.14 = 0.89 of the track, so the mark keeps ~11% of its
column as air at every width and the 56px ceiling engages once the track passes ~400px.

Two second-order notes. **The container declaration is half the fix, not plumbing for the other half**:
inline-size containment takes the column's min-content contribution to zero, which is what stops the
hero's `1fr` track being *floored* by an unbreakable word and shoving the whole grid past the clipped
edge — the same `1fr`-is-floored-at-its-own-min-content mechanic §"a shared anatomy is a grid" names on
the task-card masthead. And **`min()` is invisible to the raw-value ratchet either way**: the disable
directive that rode the bare `56` reports UNUSED the moment the value moves inside `min()` and has to come
off, exactly as it did on Everymen. The value is still ornament and still off the `--text-*` scale.

The other five heroes keep a flat size (Coven 52, the Ephemerists 44, S.N.I.D.E. 82, UA and WOW on the
scale) and that is **not** the same latent bug: every one of those names carries a space or dots to give
at, so the container yielding is enough. They are worth re-checking the day a faction is renamed, not
repainting now.

### The label tier is TWO tiers, and the content floor has one exception (#1307)

One class covered five jobs — section headings, metadata captions, status chips, counts, bylines — at 9px uppercase on 0.15em tracking in the weakest neutral, on 461 sites. Four legibility costs on the same string, and the treatment had already been found not to survive real content once: `.eyebrow-sentence` existed because *"a sentence set that way is a wall"*, which is a caption wearing a heading's clothes and a variant standing in for a rethink. **Owner ruling: two intents, not five treatments and not one.**

| Class | Intent | Size | Casing | Tracking |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `.label-heading` | **This names a region.** "COLLABORATORS · 4", "WRITE-UP", "IN PROGRESS TASKS" | `--text-md` (11px) | uppercase | 0.15em |
| `.label-caption` | **This is a small fact about the thing.** "5d ago", "5 / 20 slots", a byline, a status | `--text-lg` (12px) | normal | normal |

Being hard to read as prose is acceptable for a heading, whose job is marking where a region starts and which is one or two words by construction. It is not acceptable for a caption, which is genuinely read. `.eyebrow-sentence` is **deleted**, absorbed by the caption tier rather than kept in sync.

**The caption is nominally larger than the heading on purpose.** Uppercase reads optically larger than its nominal size, so 11px caps and 12px lowercase land at about the same weight while doing different jobs. Do not "fix" the inversion by pulling the caption to 11px: the casing is what the size is compensating for. And note where the win actually comes from — **dropping the casing and the tracking is the larger half**, since uppercase removes the ascender/descender shape that carries word recognition at small sizes and wide tracking works against reading the run as a word at all. The step from 9px is the smaller half.

**The content floor's one exception is a tier, not a site.** §4's geometry doctrine says nothing readable may sit below `--text-content` and that a container which cannot hold it is too small; the label ramp says the tier is scanned, not read. Both cannot hold for a caption carrying text people read. The floor governs **content** — the thing a container exists to hold, and the thing that can run to any length. "Make the container bigger" is an instruction you can act on for a paragraph and meaningless for a timestamp: a caption does not size its container, it rides along beside something that does. So the floor could only ever have been enforced on captions by deleting them. What the caption tier owes instead is legibility per glyph, which it buys with casing and tracking rather than with size. **The boundary is a role test, not a length test:** anything that *can* become a paragraph is content and takes `--text-content`. When a caption starts wrapping to three lines it was content in a caption's clothes, which is the mistake `.eyebrow-sentence` was minted to paper over.

**The ink is a SEAM, and it is the token — never the component (#1252).** `--label-ink` is what both classes paint; unset it is `--color-text-tertiary`, which is the ink `.eyebrow` hardcoded — so a label outside a faction frame was unchanged by the retirement. Both tiers sit under WCAG's large-text threshold, so both owe 4.5:1 and one measurement serves the pair. Measured on the #1549 instrument, the neutral clears on every neutral stock in both cascades and on all eight card sheets in **dark** — and fails on three in **light**, for a reason that is polarity rather than taste: S.N.I.D.E. **3.18:1**, Ephemerists **3.29:1** and Singularity **3.29:1** are near-black sheets in *both* themes (§6), so no light-cascade neutral can clear them. (Ephemerists was the darkest *light* sheet at 4.36:1 when this was written; #1627 moved it into the near-black group outright, which changes the number and not the ruling.) That is §3's "a colour that must differ between two factions within one theme is a faction token" arriving on the label tier. A faction frame therefore sets `--label-ink` **once on its own root** and every label underneath follows; a faction does not restyle the tier in its own component, because eight bespoke components quietly contradicting a shared class is the problem the shared class exists to prevent.

**Nothing new was minted for it, and that is the measurement's finding rather than a shortcut.** `--faction-{key}-card-muted` is already the quiet ink for this role on that sheet and clears on all eight in both cascades (worst **4.76:1**, WOW's chronicle — it was the Ephemerists plate at 4.70 until #1627 took that sheet dark), gated by rows `factionContrast.test.ts` has carried since #651.
