A second name for a value that is already measured is a name pretending to be a value (#1213), and #1302 reached the same conclusion for the praxis card's shared inks. What the guard gained instead is **structural**: every ratio in the file stays green through a `.label-caption` that hardcodes the neutral (which puts 3.18:1 back on S.N.I.D.E. with no faction able to reach it) and through one that goes back to uppercase on 0.15em (which is `.eyebrow` under a new name). Both are asserted on the declaration text, in the shape #1449 and #1413 set — a guard that measures an ink against a ground is blind to the ink not being reachable.

**Retiring it was a foundation plus per-area sweeps, and the sweeps were free.** A sweep swaps class names in `.tsx` and adds no CSS; the whole issue's stylesheet cost was the foundation's **+39 bytes gzip**, and the last sweep got some of it back by deleting `.eyebrow`. Classify each site by asking which of the two sentences it says — *this names a region* or *this is a small fact about the thing* — not by how long the string is.

**What the sweep found, and the rule it leaves behind.** The rename was the easy half. The judgement was in the inline `style` that had grown up around each site to fight the old treatment, and doing all 124 produced one rule worth keeping:

- **A hardcoded NEUTRAL goes with the class.** `color: 'var(--color-text-tertiary)'` restates what `--label-ink` already resolves to, and `--color-text-secondary` on a label was almost always somebody lifting a 9px string off the floor. Both survive a rename invisibly and both cost the frame its one seam. **23 went** — 13 tertiary, 10 secondary.
- **A SEMANTIC or FACTION ink stays.** `--color-success` on a confirmation, `--color-danger` on a withdraw link, a sheet's own `-card-muted` or `-card-accent`: signal, or contrast on a measured ground, not a patch over the old class's weakness.

Size and tracking overrides are the same tell. **11 size pins** (`text-[9px]`, `text-[10px]`, seven `text-[12px]`, one `fontSize: 'var(--text-sm)'`) existed only because 9px was too small, and **5 `letterSpacing` tunings** between 0.1em and 0.12em existed only because 0.15em was too wide — every one of them on a site that turned out to be a caption, which the class now sets to normal tracking anyway. **A widening survives, though:** the unaffiliated select card's 0.2em amplifies the heading voice rather than fighting it, and the WOW skins' 0.14em is that kit's own hand. The test is which direction the override pushes, not that there is one.

One site refused the rule and is worth knowing about: the **logged-out vote gate** takes `.label-heading`, not the caption. Its nine faction treatments override size, face, ink and — for two of them — casing, so the only thing the class still contributes is the uppercase the other seven rely on, and `.label-caption` sets `text-transform: none`. **When a class is doing nothing but supply a default its callers depend on, the tier question is which default, not which intent.**

### Button and card-meta chrome is on the same floor, and a RESTATEMENT is why it could not move one site at a time (#1783)

`.btn-primary`, `.btn-outline` and `.card-meta` sat below the tier the two role classes had already been walked up to — buttons at `--text-sm` (9px), card meta at `--text-xs` (8px), the ramp's bottom two rungs. All three are uppercase and letter-spaced, which is the **heading** register, so `--text-md` is the step they join. Not `--text-lg`: the caption is nominally the larger of the two only because it drops the casing, and pulling caps up to match the number un-matches the voices.

**Owner ruling: a button label is text a player must read TO ACT, which is a stronger case than a caption's, not a weaker one.** #769 had ruled the duel seal's buttons stay at 9px "as button chrome"; that settled a decorative seal, not the primary action chrome of the whole site, and it did not carry.

**The reason this had to move at the class and could not be swept is a shape worth recognising anywhere.** Twenty-five sites named the two smallest steps in an inline `style`, and **19 of them were RESTATEMENTS rather than overrides** — a component typing the button's own font size next to `className="btn-primary"`, the same number by intent. A restatement reads as redundant and is the opposite of redundant: an inline style wins, so every one of the 19 would have pinned its own button at 9px on the day the class moved, and *nothing warns you* — no test fails, no lint fires, the class is correct and the page is wrong. Raising one of them individually is the mirror failure: it desynchronises that button from every other button on the site. **So the only two coherent moves are "move the class and delete every restatement" and "do nothing", and raising the inline sites while leaving the class is worse than either.** They are deleted, not edited; a swap leaves the fork in place for the next move to find.

Three things the census turned up that a `className` grep would not have.

**Count with a tool, and count the twins.** The issue said 20 and the tool said 19; #1608's own headline of "461 sites" was really 241 classes plus ~121 inline twins. The instrument is `__tests__/labelTierSweep.test.ts`, which greps the **size token** rather than a class name, because the population that hides is a `CSSProperties` object laundered through a data structure. Its allow-list is the point of the file rather than an exemption from it: each surviving hit carries a written reason, and it went from 25 to 6.

**A Tailwind utility is a third twin, and it beats the class.** Eighteen `.btn-*` sites across admin carry `text-xs` (12px), which wins over the component layer — those are *overrides*, an admin density decision, and they stay. But one of them **also** carried the inline 9px, so a dormant 12px override sat under a live 9px one and the button rendered 3px below its own siblings. Deleting the inline value there activates the utility rather than landing on the class, which is the opposite of a no-op. **Before deleting a restatement, read every layer under it.**

**A hand-rolled twin resolves itself once the pair moves together.** The praxis detail's withdraw-confirm was left behind by the earlier sweep for a sharper reason than the rest: it stands *beside* a `.btn-outline` cancel, and raising one of a visible pair is worse than raising neither. The fix is not to give it a number — it is to make it wear the class it was imitating, keeping only its fill, rule, ink, pad and square corner inline. The same reasoning retired eight hand-rolled copies of the feed's action label into `.feed-action`, which is deliberately **not** `.btn-*` (weight 700, 0.1em tracking, a denser pad and a `--badge-*` fill are dress a design owns) but now reads the same `--text-md`. **Two families stepping off one token is the goal; flattening them into one class would have been a restyle nobody asked for.**

---

## 4a. Spacing

**Spacing scale** is defined as CSS variables in `index.css`. Use the variable names, not raw pixel values.

| Token         | Value |
| ------------- | ----- |
| `--space-xs`  | 4px   |
| `--space-sm`  | 8px   |
| `--space-md`  | 12px  |
| `--space-lg`  | 16px  |
| `--space-xl`  | 24px  |
| `--space-2xl` | 32px  |
| `--space-3xl` | 40px  |
| `--space-4xl` | 48px  |
| `--space-5xl` | 64px  |
| `--space-6xl` | 96px  |

The rungs through `2xl` are for spacing **within** a component; `3xl` and up are for the space **around** one — section breaks, hero padding, sticky-footer clearance. The step widens on purpose: at that size a 4px difference is not a design decision anyone is making.

**Rule:** `padding`, `margin`, and `gap` take a `--space-*` token — never a raw pixel value. If you're about to write `padding: 13` or `gap: 6`, stop and pick the nearest token. This mirrors the typography rule: the scale is the vocabulary, and a value outside it is a bug, not a nuance.

**Ties round up.** 20px sits exactly between `--space-lg` and `--space-xl`, and it is one of the most common raw values in the codebase; "nearest" does not resolve it. Round up. §4's *type wins; geometry yields* is the reason — text sizes rose site-wide in #627/#623, so containers giving up room is the wrong direction. The exception is an intentionally **asymmetric** inset (`"24px 24px 22px 20px"`), where rounding every side up flattens the asymmetry into a uniform box; there, round the tie down and keep the shape.

**Never compose with `calc()` to dodge the scale.** `calc(var(--space-2xl) + var(--space-lg))` is a 48px value wearing a disguise — it passes the linter and tells the next reader nothing. If a needed value has no rung, that is a gap in the scale to raise, not to route around.

Both scales are **global, not per-faction**. A faction picks a headline font, a colour, and an ornament — never its own type size or spacing. There are no per-faction size or spacing exceptions.

**Skins don't own type size.** A skin style object (`inputStyle`, `textareaStyle`, `markdownStyle`, and friends) carries `fontFamily`, `fontStyle`, `color`, `lineHeight` — **never `fontSize`**. The shared control owns the size, via a role class (`.content-text` / `.content-title`) or a `--text-*` token. This is the rule above made enforceable: if a skin is setting a size, the size has escaped the scale.

**The exception is a CHROME tier a skin is drawn at, and it passes through the shared helper rather than around it (#1828).** The composer's eight designs each specify their own submit-band metrics — 12 to 16px, four different tracking values, two different faces — and its label metrics differ for one kit (the Everymen at 13 where the other seven sit at 12). The rule above is about **content**: a size on an `inputStyle` is a body size escaping the floor, and nothing shared can put it back. A band is the label tier's own loudest rung, and the thing that keeps it honest is that the skin names a **rung** (`fontSize: 'var(--text-xl)'`) into a shared builder — `composerBandStyle(sizes, …)` / `composerLabelStyle(…)` — which owns the rest of the geometry and the bleed. So the test is not "did a skin write `fontSize`" but **"could it have written a number"**: a value off the ramp, or a size set on a wrapper, is still the defect. Design px land on the ramp by role — the composer's eight bands resolve to three rungs (12 / 14 / 18) rather than to one, which is the point: mapping every faction's number onto the nearest single token is the flattening `docs/agents/design-fidelity.md` was written about.

**And a helper that spreads its overrides last states a DEFAULT, not a ceiling.** `composerLabelStyle`'s 12px looked like a pin for long enough to be filed as one; every caller had simply passed no size. Before widening a seam, check whether the callers are already able to reach it — the fix for "eight files all land on 12" is usually eight call sites, not a new prop.

**A wrapper `fontSize` is the worst version of that.** A size on a *container* is inherited by every slot inside it at once, so one line silently overrides a whole surface — and no individual line looks wrong when you read it. This is what #769 was: three duel-rail skins each set `fontSize: var(--text-sm)` on their frame, and the cast roster, the next-step line and the stakes copy all rendered at 9px, half the floor, despite no line of that text naming a size. The fix inverts ownership: **each slot sets its own size and the frame sets none.** When you find a below-floor surface, look at the container before the line — the line is usually innocent.

**A token names a tier, so pick by role, not by number.** `--text-sm` is 9px, not 14px; `--text-xl` is 14px, not 20px. The Label ramp's t-shirt names read like a *size* vocabulary and invite you to reach for the nearest-looking name when tokenizing a raw value. Decide which of the three roles the string is (§4's role vocabulary), take that tier's token, and let the number land where it lands. Substituting `--text-sm` for a raw `14` is a two-tier demotion wearing a rename: it turns the lint green while making the surface materially harder to read.

**Zero is exempt.** `padding: 0` / `margin: 0` / `gap: 0` stay as written. Zero is the *absence* of spacing rather than a choice from the scale — it is unit-less and theme-invariant, so it carries none of the drift the scale exists to prevent. There is deliberately no `--space-none` token: spelling "nothing" as `var(--space-none)` is churn, not clarity. The lint rule exempts the literal `0`.

**Not covered by the rule:** ornament geometry (`width`/`height`/`top`/`inset` on decorative marks, sprocket holes, tape strips, corner brackets) is illustration, not layout spacing, and stays in raw pixels.

**Ornament spacing.** The same carve-out extends to `padding`/`margin`/`gap` *inside* an ornamental composition — the lead between stacked stencil lines, the inset of a stamp within its border, the offset of a taped label. Rounding those to the nearest rung reflows the composition, and §6's "do not regularize card sizes" applies to an archetype's internal rhythm as much as to its outer dimensions. Such a value keeps its raw pixels behind the same per-line hatch ornament `fontSize` uses. The test is unchanged: spacing that positions **layout** takes a token; spacing that positions **illustration** is ornament. When genuinely unsure, it is layout — the carve-out is narrow, and a gutter between two paragraphs is never ornament.

**An on-scale value can never be ornament.** The carve-out exists because *rounding* an off-scale value reflows the composition. A value already sitting on a rung is not rounded at all, so that justification is absent and the claim is empty. Before hatching, check the number: `padding: 4` is `--space-xs` and simply takes the token, however ornamental its surroundings. A legitimate claim is **off-scale** *and* **in register with raw ornament geometry**. Two recurring shapes qualify: a `paddingLeft: 46` clearing a *drawn* margin rule at `left: 32`, where rounding runs body text into the line; and a **ring-stroke inset** (`padding: 2`/`3`/`6` on an avatar band, gilt frame or seal) where the inset *is* the drawn band — 4px is a 33–100% thicker ring that visibly eats the inner disc. Both are correctness, not taste.

**This rule does NOT extend to the type scale.** A `--text-*` token names a **tier**, not merely a number: `--text-base` means *Label tier*. A 10px stamp is not label-tier text — it is a stamp that happens to be 10px, and tokenizing it would couple it to a tier it was never part of, so retuning that tier later would silently drag the ornament along. Spacing tokens are near-purely dimensional, which is why the on-scale rule binds them and not type. **Ornament type keeps its raw value even when the number happens to sit on a rung.**

### The ornament escape hatch

Some type is illustration rather than text: a marker scrawl, a stamp, a tape label, a punch-card header, a condensed poster face, hand-lettering on a pinned index card. It carries a raw pixel size on purpose, because rounding it to the nearest token would flatten one archetype into another. §6's "do not regularize card sizes" applies to type too.

The test is what the size is *doing*, not how big it is. Display-face type whose optical size is not the text scale is ornament even at 13px; anything a player actually reads is content even at 30px. **When you are genuinely unsure, it is ornament** — the content-text floor is a rule about reading, not about every number in the file.

Ornament that keeps a raw value must say so **at the site**, so the exemption is legible to the next reader instead of hiding behind a filename in a list. This is a **two-phase** state, because a file's `fontSize` axis and its spacing axis migrate on different schedules (#623 and #750):

**Phase 1 — file still grandfathered (the rule is off for it).** Annotate with a plain comment. An `eslint-disable` directive here would be an *unused* directive, since the rule never fires on a listed file:

```js
// ornament: hand-lettered Caveat — handwriting on the board, not typeset copy.
fontSize: 19,
```

**Phase 2 — file delisted (the rule is on for it).** The comment becomes the directive, and the exemption turns per-line and self-documenting instead of per-file and invisible:

```js
// eslint-disable-next-line local/no-raw-style-values -- ornament: hand-lettered Caveat.
fontSize: 19,
```

A file only moves to phase 2 when it is clean on **both** axes — no un-annotated raw `fontSize` *and* no raw `padding`/`margin`/`gap`. Tokenizing only the type leaves it in phase 1.

**Enforcement.** The `local/no-raw-style-values` ESLint rule fails the build on a raw numeric `fontSize`/`padding`/`margin`/`gap` in an inline style. Files not yet migrated are grandfathered in `frontend/.eslint-legacy-raw-styles.txt`. **That list only ever shrinks** — migrating a file means deleting its line; no file may ever be added to it. The hatch above is what keeps that literally true: ornament is not a permanent residue on the list, it is a per-line directive on a delisted file. The list reaches **empty** when #750 (the spacing sweep) closes and the last file moves to phase 2. **It is empty now** — so a newly-flagged violation is fixed in place or hatched per-line, never grandfathered.

**What the rule sees.** A raw value is a raw value whichever notation carries it, so the rule covers three shapes: a numeric inline style (`padding: 6`), a length string including `rem`/`em` (`padding: '0.6rem 1.2rem'`), and an **arbitrary Tailwind spacing utility** (`mt-[6px]`, `px-[10px]`) — the last of these leaves the style object entirely and so needs a separate `className` check (#763). It walks ternaries, `&&` chains and template literals, because the recurring lesson of #770/#789 is that *any* indirection hid the value from a literal-only check. **A sign is an indirection too**: `marginLeft: -3.5` is a `UnaryExpression` wrapping a literal, not a literal, so for four issues the ratchet caught `3.5` and waved the negative through — and negative margins are common in exactly the place this section cares about, pulling ornament back over a padded edge. The rule unwraps `-`/`+` before judging the operand (#1233), which leaves `-0` reading as `0` and staying exempt. **A negated token is spelled `calc(-1 * var(--space-lg))`** — the one calc composition §4a's warning above does not cover, because it names a rung rather than routing around one.

**What it deliberately does not see**, each a judgement rather than an oversight:

| Gap | Why it stays open |
| --- | --- |
| `text-sm` / `text-xs` on prose | Needs to know prose from chrome; a className carries no role signal. `text-sm` is right on a timestamp and wrong on a paragraph. Review-only. |
| `text-[13px]` | A `--text-*` token names a **tier**. Flagging arbitrary type mechanically would pin ornament to a tier it was never part of — the coupling this section forbids. |
| `calc(...)` | Named above; composing around the scale is a review rule, not a regex. |
| `w-`/`h-`/`top-`/`inset-` | Ornament geometry, already carved out above. |
| `marginLeft: -bleed` | A minus sign over an **identifier** or inside a template literal. Reading it needs flow analysis; the sign itself is closed (#1233), the indirection under it is not. |
| ~~A token at the **wrong tier**~~ | **Closed for the text family by the tier arm below (#1819).** Still open for every other family — a faction accent used as a page ground is the same disease and has no rule. |
| A dressed surface **outside** an `archetypes/` name | The tier arm scopes by directory, because the directory name *is* this repo's dispatch signal. `editPraxis/waiting/`, `components/praxisCard/desktop/` and the duel seal confirms are faction-dressed and unnamed; hand-listing them would turn a convention into a maintained enumeration. Renaming the directory is the cheap fix. |

**The colour arm (#1853).** Everything above judges raw *numbers*. A colour is neither a bare number nor a spacing prop, so for a long time every colour literal in the tree linted clean — which is how #1851's pair of hand-rolled `rgba(0,0,0,0.15)` dropdown shadows survived a green run over both, and why three separate notes in §3 above had to say "the ratchet covers px, not colour". `local/no-raw-colour-values` is the second arm, with its own shrink-only list at `frontend/.eslint-legacy-raw-colours.txt` (seeded at **61 files / 140 violations**; #1609 burns it down — **8 files / 13 violations**, with groups 1 and 2 done, group 3 decided per site, and the print-register class cleared). It reads three shapes:

- a colour function in a string — `boxShadow: '0 4px 12px rgba(0,0,0,0.15)'`, plus `rgb()`/`hsl()`/`hsla()`;
- a raw hex in an inline style — `style={{ color: '#dc2626' }}`, which is `--color-danger`'s **light** value painting light red on a dark ground;
- a **stock Tailwind colour utility** — `text-red-600`, `border-red-300`. Same blind spot as `text-sm`: a value wearing a class name. The repo's own utilities (`text-ink`, `bg-surface`) are `var()`-backed in `tailwind.config.ts` and stay silent by construction.

**A scrim and a cast shadow are two tokens, and the dark theme is what proves it (#1609, group 2).** Nineteen surfaces had hand-rolled their lift as `rgba(0,0,0,α)` at eleven alphas between 0.04 and 0.5, and four more had reached for `--color-overlay-strong` — the modal scrim — as a shadow colour, which laundered the same defect past the rule entirely. They are not one token, because they want opposite things of `[data-theme="dark"]`: a scrim answers *"the page behind is unavailable"* and reads correctly on a near-black page **unchanged**, while a cast shadow at the same alpha is a black smudge on a black ground and disappears. That is why `--color-overlay-strong` has no dark counterpart and never should, and why `--color-cast-shadow` (0.25 → **0.7**) does — the same lift `--dropdown-shadow` already makes, generalised. **The token is a colour, not a whole shadow**: geometry stays at the call site (`0 8px 28px var(--color-cast-shadow)`), because blur and offset are a component's business and only the paint was ever off-theme — the same split `.danger-edge` makes for a border. Two rungs, not eleven: `-soft` is the ambient lift of paper resting *on* the page rather than floating above it (the Albescent invite letter, whose 0.06 would become a grey halo at the floating rung). Mint a third when a surface needs one — and note that `-weak`/`-medium` were once declared alongside `-strong`, read by nothing, and deleted.

**One scrim for every overlay, and that ruling was already in the stylesheet.** `.filter-factions__scrim` records it: an outlier at 0.55 was collapsed to `--color-overlay-strong` because *"two modal sheets disagreeing about how dark the page behind is unavailable looks is the drift a token exists to stop."* Seven more dialogs were still disagreeing — three warm `radial-gradient(ellipse …, rgba(26,18,9,0.30 → 0.66))` vignettes on the letter-popup family (at **two** depths inside `LevelUpPopup` alone), and three neutral `radial-gradient(circle, rgba(0,0,0,0.45 → 0.75))` on the metatask and collab dialogs. All flat now. A vignette is a nice thing to lose; five different answers to one question is a worse thing to keep.

**Where a token exists, a percentage of it beats a new rung.** The strays this pass turned up were not elevation at all, and each had an owner already in the tree: the collab roster's warn veil was Tailwind's amber-500 sitting under **eight different faction inks**, and now mixes `banner.tone` — the same ink the border and the text read, so it cannot drift from them; the Field Desk's dot field was a fixed black that simply vanished on `#13121a`, and now mixes `--color-text-primary`, which is how the Everymen and S.N.I.D.E. wall grounds already draw theirs in `index.css`. **`color-mix` on a themed token is one value that serves both themes**, and the rule stays silent on it by construction — it reports raw literals, and a mix of a `var()` is not one.

**Two shapes still launder a cast past the rule, and both are the property, not the value.** `filter: drop-shadow(0 4px 8px rgba(0,0,0,0.25))` is not reported because `filter` is not a `COLOUR_PROP` — the top-praxis ribbon carried that in two files verbatim, neither of them on the legacy list. And a module constant (`const HAIRLINE = 'rgba(0,0,0,0.10)'`) is Gap D's indirection wearing paint. Neither is a hole in the regex; both mean **grep the file you just migrated**, because passing the rule and carrying no raw colour are different facts. Class 1 hit both again and added a third: **a property name the rule does not judge.** `EverymenProfileBody` paints raw `rgba()` through `pageOverlay`, `barTrack` and a `dividerColor=` JSX attribute — a `COLOUR_PROPS` set is an enumeration of *style* keys, and a component's own prop named after a colour is outside it. None of those three is reportable, so none can ever go on the shrinking list; the list undercounts and says so in its own header.

**The question that decides an ornament colour is not "is this ornament" — it is "does `index.css` already own it" (#1609, group 3).** The paragraph above says a one-off ornament keeps its raw value, and read alone that is an invitation to argue every site on taste. It is not a taste question, because the *cost* of the two answers is asymmetric and measured: a site whose right answer is an **existing** token is free — the swap happens in a `.tsx`, most of them lazy archetypes, and the blocking stylesheet does not move. A site whose right answer is a **new** token costs the one sheet that blocks first paint, and §6's lazy-ornament rule already says that is the wrong place to spend. So the sweep minted nothing, and the 66 violations sorted themselves: 39 had a name waiting and moved; 27 would have needed a new one and stayed, with the reason on the list.

**What "a name was waiting" turned out to mean, four times over, is worth knowing before the next sweep prices this work.** It was almost never "a `--faction-*` tier that ornament should not touch". It was: a token's own value **hardcoded** (`#d8d6c8` is `--faction-snide-card-muted`'s light value, `#cfcdbf` its dark — group 1's defect wearing a faction name); one ornament **drawn N ways** (the Singularity raster has been a token since #723 and eight surfaces read it, while a ninth hand-rolled it at 0.015 and a tenth drew it in *white* on a green-phosphor terminal); a **module constant** hiding the whole tile (`const green = "#4ade80"` reaching four style objects and two hover handlers as an Identifier, which no colour rule can see); and a **rim that could not flip** (a fixed black hairline on `--fdl-disc`, which goes `#faf6ee → #1a1712`, so in dark it was black on near-black and drew nothing). None of those are ornament. They are the raw-colour disease inside files that happen to be ornamental, and the ornament framing is what let them sit.

**A raw colour can be a live AA failure, and the sweep is how you find it.** Three sites put a light ink on `--faction-snide-pink`: white at **3.50:1** twice and `--faction-snide-paper` at **3.10:1** on the Field Desk's primary action. The ink that clears is `--faction-snide-on-accent`, at 5.38:1 — declared, measured, and already read by the five *other* surfaces that fill with that pink. So the outliers were the bug, and the tell was available without rendering anything: **read the hue's `-on-accent` / `-on-fill` line before inking on a fill**, exactly as #1932 says. The 3.10 was reached through a token and so was invisible to the colour rule; it came out of grepping every caller of the fill once the first raw one was found.

**When the residue is one metaphor, the next move is one token — and it may still be right to defer it.** The largest class of the 27 survivors was a single drawing: the flyposter's **flat offset print shadow** (`8px 10px 0` down to `1px 2px 0` — zero blur, hard edge) at nine strengths between 0.2 and 0.55. That is the same eleven-alpha shape group 2 collapsed, so the instinct to collapse it was right; what stopped it was that a print shadow is *not* elevation, so it cannot borrow `--color-cast-shadow` (whose 0.25 → 0.7 dark lift would swell a printed mark), and a new rung costs the blocking sheet bytes it did not have. **Deferring is a decision with an upgrade path, not an omission** — it belonged on the list in those words, so the next reader inherited the diagnosis instead of re-deriving it. It was taken up once #2073 moved ornament motion off the critical path and the room arrived; the three entries below are what the taking-up decided.

### One NAME was the token; then the STRENGTH became one too (#1609 → #2302, class 1)

**Mint the name, keep the per-site value — a sweep that also changes the look is two changes wearing one PR.** The list's own upgrade path read *"collapsing the eight alphas onto ONE print token"*, and taken literally that is a visual change at up to sixteen of seventeen sites, shipped by an agent with no browser. So `--color-print-offset` is one name and nothing else: each site writes `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-print-offset) 32%, transparent)` at the strength it already printed at, and the change **repaints no pixel**. That is not a compromise, it is the same split made twice before for the same reason — group 2 kept blur and offset at the call site because *"only the paint was off-theme"*, and `shared.tsx`'s byline rule is a percentage of `currentColor`. **How hard a particular mark is printed is geometry's kind of fact, not paint's.** Collapsing the nine strengths was left as the honest next move and a one-line change — and #2302 took it, below.

**Collapse onto ONE first; earn a tier back by looking.** #2302 struck all seventeen registers at **40%** (owner's ruling, 2026-08-22). What had stalled the call four times was the framing *pick one of nine*, which genuinely needs eyes on seventeen surfaces; the census dissolved it. 40% was already six of seventeen and 35–45% was ten of seventeen, so the middle is one strength already; and **strength did not track offset distance** — three marks at `8px 10px` printed at 32, 40 and 55 — so there was no system being preserved by keeping nine. Starting uniform turns the question into defending two exceptions rather than ranking nine values, and each is a one-line revert. **A visible change shipped deliberately is not a regression, but it has to be announced**: four light marks (20/22/30/32) darkened and two heavy ones (50/55) lightened, and the PR says which six surfaces moved, because `main` auto-deploys and an unexplained repaint reads as an accident. If a lighter or harder impression turns out to be needed, it is minted as a **named tier — at most three** — never a percentage typed at a call site: nine values grew one call site at a time, which is exactly how they grow back.

**A printed offset is theme-INVARIANT, and that is an argument rather than an omission.** A cast deepens at night because the ground behind it went black and a 0.25 smudge vanishes into it. An offset register has no such ground problem to solve: it is *part of the drawing*, struck on surfaces whose own rulings already fix them in both cascades — S.N.I.D.E.'s press constants (`-ink`/`-paper`/`-acid`, "the ink that prints on an acid ground stays `#14110b` in both themes") and Everymen's poster frame, which "stays the colour it was printed in" (#1187). So `--color-print-offset` has **no dark half**, deliberately, and the reasoning sits at the declaration where the next reader will go hunting for one. **Where a printed offset genuinely does need a night answer, the answer is geometry, not paint**: `--faction-snide-note-wall-shadow` trades its offset for a hairline plus a deep drop, because an offset against black is invisible — and geometry lives at the call site, so that is not this token's decision to make. Say which of the two a new mark is before reaching for either.

**The rule's report is a FLOOR on a class, not its extent — three of the seventeen were invisible to it.** Fourteen were reported. The other three were the laundering shapes named above, and finding them meant grepping the *drawing* (`Npx Npx 0` followed by a raw colour) rather than the rule: a `filter: drop-shadow(...)`, a `shadow=` JSX prop that `TaskCrown` applies as a filter, and a `const CARD_SHADOW`. Two of the three sat in files that were on no list at all, so no ratchet would ever have gone green *or* red over them. **When you clear a class, count the metaphor and not the report** — and leave the count somewhere, which here is `printOffsetToken.test.ts`: it pins the single strength (nine of them until #2302) and a per-file site count, pins the absent dark half, and fails on a zero-blur register that regrows with a raw colour in any of the four spellings. The one distinction that regex has to make is the same one the class taxonomy makes: **a register is offset sideways and a printed RULE is not**, so a non-zero X offset is what separates class 1 from class 5's `0 2px 0` masthead underline, which stays raw.

**The tier arm (#1819).** The colour arm asks whether a value is a token. `local/no-global-ink-on-faction-surface` asks whether it is the **right** token for the surface it is written on — the question no literal rule can reach, because `var(--color-text-tertiary)` is declared in `index.css` and reads as correctly tokenized to every other check we run. Inside `archetypes/`, `mobileArchetypes/` and `components/factionMarks/`, the global `--color-text-*` family is banned; those surfaces read `--label-ink`, `--link-ink`, or the matching `factionCssVar`. Own rule id, own shrink-only list at `frontend/.eslint-legacy-faction-ink.txt` (seeded at **20 files / 121 violations**).

**Wrong because unreachable, which is why no ratio ever caught it.** §4's label tier paints `var(--label-ink)`, a seam a faction frame repoints **once on its own root**. An inline restatement of the neutral wins over the class and takes that seam out of reach — for all eight factions at once — and `factionContrast.test.ts` stays green throughout, because it measures the token and not whether anything reads it. On the three sheets that are near-black in **both** cascades that is the whole difference between a fixable number and an unfixable one: S.N.I.D.E. 2.19:1, Singularity 2.27:1, the Ephemerists plate 2.01:1, and no light-cascade neutral can ever clear a near-black ground.

**The scope is the difficulty, and a global ban would be the wrong fix** — the neutral tokens are correct on neutral chrome, which is most of the app. Two things settle it. The repo already names its faction-dispatched surfaces in the *filesystem*: `factions/manifest.ts` lazy-loads one module per faction out of `archetypes/` (desktop) or `mobileArchetypes/` (the separate mobile tree, #494), and `components/factionMarks/` holds the ornaments those mount. Every file under those names is a faction-named archetype — including `Default*`, which is `na`/Unaffiliated's own identity (ADR-0030), not an escape hatch — or a helper shared *by* archetypes and mounted on nothing else. And the tempting alternative, "any file that calls `factionCssVar`", is **wrong**: 60+ files do, including `Sidebar.tsx` and `ConfirmDialog.tsx`, which reach for one accent and are otherwise correctly neutral. That scope would ban the right token on the surfaces it is right for.

**A `??` fallback is exempted in the rule, not grandfathered on the list.** `inkColor ?? 'var(--color-text-tertiary)'` applies only when a skin supplied no ink, which *is* the legitimate neutral default; it is never going to migrate, and a line nobody can ever delete makes a shrink-only list dishonest. The left half of the same operator is still reported — the exemption is the fallback, not the operator.

**Two-thirds of the seeded list is a rename, not a repaint.** Thirteen of the twenty files are `Default*` archetypes whose ground *is* the app's neutral paper: there, `--color-text-tertiary` → `--label-ink` changes no pixel and buys the seam back. The seven dressed files are where the tier is actually wrong. **And the family is the unit, not one name in it** — #1819's own grep was for `tertiary` and walked past two live `--color-text-secondary` sites in the very file it was about, one of them the frozen notice's only way out.

**This is a different failure from a raw `14`.** The number is merely off-scale; the colour is off-*theme*. A raw colour in a component has no `[data-theme="dark"]` counterpart and cannot be given one without a `dark ? a : b` ternary, which §3 forbids outright — so every entry on that list is a surface already wrong in dark mode or about to be. Three exemptions are deliberate: `index.css` (where colour values belong — the rule is `.ts`/`.tsx` only), colour-**math** helpers like `utils/contrast.ts` (they parse colour by definition), and tests (a contrast guard names the hex it measured). **Ornament colour gets §4a's judgement, not a carve-out**: Snide's `rgba(182,255,46,0.055)` ghost lettering names a one-off ornament rather than a tier, exactly as `text-[13px]` does — a site that stays raw stays *on the list with its reason written down*, never behind an inline disable, because an inline disable hides the decision from the next reader.

---

## 5. Layout

Every logged-in page follows this shell:

```
Nav (sticky, frosted glass)
Watercolor Background (absolute, behind content)
Body: Main Content (flex: 1) + Sidebar (minmax 280–340px)
```

The sidebar contains: character card, active tasks panel, recent activity panel, propose-a-task button. Some pages add contextual panels (e.g., faction standings on Players, pending requests on Updates).

**Exceptions:** Submit Proof and Propose Task forms drop the sidebar for a single-column writing layout.

### A full-bleed child negates its container's OWN token, and that makes it shared (#1828)

The only way a child of a padded column reaches its parent's edge is a negative margin of the padding — not "a value off the scale that happens to be about right", but *the same token running the other way*: `margin: 0 calc(-1 * var(--space-2xl)) calc(-1 * var(--space-2xl))`. Say it that way and the bleed cannot drift when the column's inset changes; say it as `-32` and it is a second copy of a number.

**Which is exactly why the bleed belongs to the layout rather than to the skin.** The composer's submit band was drawn full-bleed for seven of eight factions, and three of them had built it by hand: one negated the sides and faked the bottom by setting `paddingBottom: 0` on the content column (a different geometry — the sheet loses its inset for everything, not just the band), one negated three sides correctly, and one wrote `width: 100%` and stopped at the inset on all three. Nobody had noticed, because each looks right in its own file. The container already knows its insets, so it is the container's hook that hands them out (`ComposerSizes.padX` / `.padBottom`) and one shared builder that spends them.

Two things the shared version then gets for free, and they are the two the hand-rolled ones missed: the band's **top rule takes the sheet's frame** (the Everymen's is `--faction-everymen-composer-frame`, gold in light and red-deep in dark — *not* `--everymen-frame`, which is the ink the inner panels are ruled in), and the sheet's existing `overflow: hidden` rounds the band's bottom corners for it, so no skin restates the radius. **When you find the same negative margin in three files, the number is not the duplication — the container's inset is.**

### A component's ground belongs to the component, not the viewport (#1028)

A faction skin that wants a full-page ground reaches for `position: fixed; inset: 0`, and it is wrong every time: it paints over the app. **Six of the eight v2 task-detail skins shipped that way and had to be corrected** — owner QA on #1055, #1057 and #1065 caught the last of them. The watercolor background and the page shell must still show *around* the component; a skin owns its own column, never the window. This is §10's "no solid color backgrounds on the page" seen from the other side, and the correct shape is a layer inside the component's column, inset to land exactly on its own sheet.

**The stacking half bites next, and it caught UA and S.N.I.D.E. independently: a positioned ornament at `z-index: 0` paints ABOVE static copy.** The content column is what establishes the stacking context, and the copy inside it is unpositioned — so `z-index: 0` on a positioned overlay is not "the bottom of the pile", it is above every static sibling. Two ways out, and which one is correct is decided by what is underneath:

- `z-index: -1` puts the ornament behind the column's own content. It renders *nowhere* if the thing beneath paints its own opaque background.
- An explicit blend — `mix-blend-mode: multiply` in light, `screen` in dark, at a trimmed opacity — keeps the ornament on top and keeps the ink under it legible. This is the one an opaque sheet needs.

`z-index: 0` is neither, and it reads as if it were both. When an ornament lands over copy, the bug is almost always here rather than in the opacity.

### The phone's bottom edge is spoken for, and `position: fixed` does not escape it (#1566)

Two separate rules meet at the bottom 3.5rem of a phone viewport, and the filter bottom sheet broke both at once — on every filter surface in the app, since `OptionPicker` is shared by tasks, praxes and updates.

**A fixed child cannot climb out of an ancestor's stacking context, and `z-index` is what makes that invisible.** The sheet sits at `z-index: 40` and `MobileTabBar` at `10`, so the sheet looks like it wins. It does not: `ShellContent`'s mobile region is `relative` with `z-index: 5`, which opens a stacking context, so *everything* inside it — the sheet at 40, its scrim at 39 — composites at 5 against a bar sitting at 10 in the **root** context. The bar paints over the sheet. That is why the report was "Done is cut off" rather than "Done is behind the tab bar": the number in the rule says the opposite of what renders. **When two fixed elements disagree about who is on top, read the ancestors before you read the z-indexes** — and note that raising the region's z-index is the wrong lever, because it puts ordinary page content over the bar.

**Bottom chrome is a reservation, not a paint order.** The right fix is for anything pinned to the bottom of a phone viewport to reserve the strip the tab bar occupies. That strip is two things summed — the bar's height, then the home-indicator inset beneath it — and it was written out longhand as `calc(3.5rem + env(safe-area-inset-bottom))` in four files with no name before the sheet became the fifth consumer and reserved nothing at all. It is `--tab-bar-clearance` now, composed from `--tab-bar-height`. Two properties of it are worth knowing. **The height is measured, not declared** — the bar has no height rule for the token to read, so it is five `py-2` tabs at `--text-lg` under a 2px rule, and if that type moves nothing catches the drift. And **the `env()` half is currently inert**: without `viewport-fit=cover` on the viewport meta the browser already excludes the safe area from the layout viewport, so the inset resolves to `0px` — correct today, and correct on the day someone sets `viewport-fit`, which is exactly why it stays in the sum.

The corollary at the sheet: **a `max-height` measured from `bottom: 0` is not the height it claims.** `70vh` was spending its last 3.5rem on chrome, so it grows by the clearance to go back to meaning 70vh of *usable* panel. And a panel with a pinned action gives the scroll to the **list**, not to itself — the label row and the Done button stay put, a long facet can never push the action below the fold, and the bottom padding stays out of a scrollport, where browsers have a long history of dropping it on a flex column.

### On a phone, the page's primary action sits above the fold — and no test can check it (#2234, #2244, #2130)

**The convention: on a phone, the thing the page exists for is on the first screenful.** Not the page's title, not its furniture, not a panel reporting that there is nothing to report — the verb, or the content the player came for. It is a layout rule and it is reviewed by eye.

**It is reviewed by eye because it cannot be reviewed any other way here, and that was ruled rather than assumed.** The obvious guard — a check that fails when a primary action falls outside the viewport — is not buildable in this repo. The frontend harness is `renderToStaticMarkup` in node: no DOM, no layout, no viewport, so nothing in CI can know where the fold is, and PR CI runs no real browser. A guard would have to be a browser test, which is a much larger piece of work than the defect justifies. So the rule lives here, where a reviewer of mobile work meets it, and **a PR that touches a mobile surface says which surfaces it checked and what it found — including the ones it left alone.** A sweep whose coverage is not stated cannot be trusted, because the failure mode is silence.

**Three reports, two mechanisms.** The first is *the action is under the chrome*: `ShellContent`'s mobile region opens a stacking context at `z-index: 5`, so a `fixed inset-0` overlay raised from page content composites below `MobileHeader` and `MobileTabBar` at 10 whatever it declares — the metatask picker's Attach button was off the bottom of a sheet that IS the viewport, with nothing to scroll to (#2244), and the invite letter lost ENLIST off both ends at once (#2130). The escape is `drawAtRoot`; the diagnosis is the section above. The second is *the action is pushed down by furniture*: the Updates page spent 236 of the 742 usable CSS px above the tab bar on a bordered, titled, collapsible panel whose whole message was that nothing was waiting, and the reporter concluded the notification system was broken (#2234). **Read the second one as an instance of §1.4.** A control a player cannot use is hidden rather than disabled; a panel about work a player does not have is the same statement about the same screen, and on a phone it is not free — it is paid for out of the only screenful there is.

**Two shapes that look like violations and are not.** A **form's** submit sits at the end of the form: you fill it, then you file it, and lifting the button above the fields would be the defect. And a **shortcut** is not the page's primary action — the Field Desk's "Find a task" and "Cast votes" land below the identity block and the in-progress list, and they may, because `MobileTabBar` carries the same two destinations permanently on screen. The test is whether the player has another way to the same place *without scrolling*. If the answer is no, the action is buried.

**When the fix is a reorder rather than a lift, it may not be yours to make.** The seven bespoke faction bodies put the join rail last in source order — correct for the desktop grid, and on a phone it lands after the manifesto, the roster, the task row and the praxis gallery. `DefaultFactionBody` pins its verb with `MobileStickyBar` instead, which is the shape this rule wants, but the divergence is recorded as a decision (ADR-0078 and the `ponytail:` on that join block), and the collapsed grid's order is a `@media (max-width: 860px)` rule in `index.css`. **A convention does not override a recorded decision** — raise it as an issue and name both.


### A card that owns its width needs a column that CENTRES, not one that stretches (#1964) — and below 768px it no longer owns one (#2763)

§10's "do not regularize card sizes" and a `flex-col` disagree, and on the phone the column wins by default. Every faction task card sets its own `width` (`size.cardWidth` — 340 on the phone, 384 on the desktop) with `maxWidth: 100%` under it, so it is narrower than a single-column results list. `align-items` defaults to `stretch`, which makes the card's wrapper full-bleed; the card then draws at its own width *inside* that wrapper and lands flush against the left edge. The report reads as "the cards are narrower than the filter bar", and the tempting fix — widen the card, or make it fluid — is the one the style guide forbids. **`align-items: center` is the compatible fix: the card keeps the width it chose and the column stops pretending it chose 100%.**

Which columns take it is decided by whether the CHILD carries a width. A praxis card does not — it fills whatever it is given, so the praxis lists stay stretched, and a metatask seal is the same, which is why the mobile browse applies the class conditionally rather than to the whole results column. The three lists that stack a fixed-width `<TaskCard>` (mobile browse, and the mobile Default and WOW profile task tabs) centre. **A flex-WRAP container is a separate question and is deliberately left alone**: the faction bodies and the desktop board wrap left-flush on purpose, and centring them would recentre a partial last row on the desktop, which is the "intentional chaos" this section is protecting.

**BELOW 768px THIS SECTION IS SUPERSEDED, AND THE PREMISE IS WHAT MOVED (#2763, owner ruling 2026-08-27).** On a phone a task card FILLS its content column: every archetype's mobile `cardWidth` is `"100%"`, Albescent's wrapper drops `fit-content` at the same breakpoint, and `.task-card-row > *` takes the whole line under `@media (max-width: 767px)`. So the three columns above stretch again — the centring was a fix for a width the card no longer has, and `align-items: center` sizes an item to its CONTENT, which would now shrink a card that asked for the line. **`align-items: center` remains the desktop answer** and nothing above 768px changes, the flex-WRAP carve-out included.

Why the fix this section forbids is the right one there and only there: below the breakpoint the row wraps to one card per line whatever width the card chose, so there is no ragged row left to protect — a 340px card in a 552px column is not one identity among nine, it is a card that missed the margin, which is how it was reported on every faction page at once. **A cap was considered and rejected**: 384px would reintroduce the same left-flush gap between 384 and 767, which is the bug.

The praxis half of the same report is `.praxis-gallery > * { max-width: 50% }` (#2229), now wrapped in `@media (min-width: 768px)`. Its own note — "50% never bites a full row" — is a desktop statement: on a phone the gallery is always one card per row, so the row is always under-filled and the cap always fires. `--praxis-card-basis` is untouched; it says where a DESKTOP row wraps, which is a different question.

### A bordered panel may not sit directly inside another bordered panel (#2131)

**Framing is how a surface says "this is one object".** Two frames, one inside the other, say it twice about a thing that is once. Three say it three times, and the report that names it says "a window in a window in a window" — which is what the Cozy Coven character profile was: an identity band at `2px solid --faction-coven-slip-border`, the shared `CredentialCard` inside it at `2px solid --faction-coven-card-accent`, and the progression panel beside the card at `1.5px solid --faction-coven-slip-border` again. Concentric, and all of it pink.

The rule is **faction-blind**. Coven is the loud case because its edge is a high-contrast pink, but a kit that frames its band in a neutral hairline is doing the identical thing quietly, and the day someone raises that hairline's contrast it becomes the same screenshot. So this is a layout rule, not a Coven note.

**The inner panel is normally the one that gives way** — it drops to a background fill, or to a single-side hairline divider, which is not a frame and is always allowed. **Which one gives way is decided by which frame survives being moved.** On the Coven profile it went the other way round: the card kept its edge, because the card is the object being looked at and it renders framed on the FieldDesk roster and the creation preview too, so its frame is a property of the component rather than of this page. The band gave way instead, and it lost nothing — it already carried `--faction-coven-ward-card` and a 16px radius, which is a panel. **A ground and a radius are enough to be a panel; the edge is a third statement of the same thing.** The elevation shadow stays, for the same reason: a shadow is depth, not an outline.

Two devices the rule does NOT catch, and the distinction is the device's job rather than its shape. **A portrait ring is a ring, not a panel border** — the pink circle around the Coven avatar is a mount for a face, doing what a mat does in a picture frame, and it stays even though it reads as a fourth circle in the screenshot. Same for a badge medallion. And **a `border-left` accent rule is a rule**: the Everymen progression panel's 5px gold edge marks a side, it does not enclose. If it encloses, it is a frame and this rule applies.

### A row of fixed hit boxes yields its SPACING, then its line — never its shapes (#2236)

The Ephemerists vote plate is five discs at 44px (50 at rank 5) with a 24px gap and 24px of inline padding: 370px intrinsic, on a plate that is 301px on a phone and can be 232px inside a praxis card at its 280px floor. They were ordinary flex items, so they did the only thing flexbox offers — they shrank on the inline axis alone, and the report was **"these should be circles"**. It is the same defect wherever a widget draws a row of round or square targets, and the order of who gives way is fixed. **The disc never does**: 44×44 is non-negotiable (§6), and an oval fails it in one axis while still measuring 44 in the other, which is why nothing in the type or contrast guards can see it. The **gap and the padding** give way first, the **line** gives way second (`flex-wrap`, so the last disc drops rather than five of them going oval), and only a design ruling may touch the target.

**Which query answers is decided by what actually varies, and for a card-mounted widget that is the container.** The vote UI renders inside a praxis card (`flex: 1 1 394px`, floor 280) *and* on a praxis-detail page, so a viewport media query would be right about the phone and wrong about a narrow card on a wide screen — the failure would survive the fix on the surface nobody screenshotted. `container-type: inline-size` on the plate with an `@container` on the row is the honest unit, and this is the repo's first one: **reach for it when the same component is mounted at two widths on one screen, and keep `@media` for the page shell, which really is the viewport.** Two consequences worth knowing before you copy it. The query needs a **container element that is not the one being styled**, which the plate already had (a brass mount wrapping the row). And the values have to **leave the inline style to get into a stylesheet at all** — an inline `gap` beats every rule that could yield it, so moving them is part of the fix and not a tidy-up; say so where they used to live, or the next reader reads the component and believes the spacing is unconditional.

---

## 6. Faction Card Archetypes

**Core principle:** Each faction's tasks use a completely different card archetype. The card type IS the faction identity. All cards display: task name, faction name, point value, level requirement.

**Task cards v2 (ADR-0055 / ADR-0056) draw the level themselves.** The redesigned task cards are the one archetype family that does *not* reach for `LevelGem`: every design in the v2 kit draws the level as a numeral in its own display face — under tally strokes on the Ephemerists plate, over a braid on the Coven slip, beside a dashed rule on the Everymen bill. The gem stays the shared shape everywhere else (rosters, detail pages, praxis surfaces), and the reasoning below is unchanged for those; on this one surface the whole point of the redesign was that the hero row belongs to the faction. Two other v2 rules ride along and apply to every card in the family: it is **one responsive component** (`useFormFactor` picks a size set — never a second file, never a fixed-px grid), and it shows **base points** with a `×multiplier` badge gated on `isNeutralMultiplier`, which is invisible at `era_1` and lights up on its own the day an era ships a non-1.0 modifier.

**Reading a design kit: four things in a `.jsx` that are not part of the design.** Four waves of kits now agree on these, so treat them as translation rules rather than judgement calls.

- **A skin never mutilates user-authored text.** S.N.I.D.E.'s task-card design redacted two words of the task's own brief as black bars — true to the ransom-note metaphor, and vetoed on sight (#1023): the description belongs to the player who wrote it, and no amount of fitting the archetype earns a skin the right to edit it. The line is between *restyling* and *destroying*. The same card's headline slices the title across four typefaces and that is fine, because every word survives and is readable. When a design proposes eating content, keep the ornament that carries the same idea and drop the part that touches the string — here, a decorative strip of redaction blocks used as a section rule says "censor" without censoring anything.

- **An A/B prop is canvas experimentation, not a contract.** WOW's task-card design takes a `ctaGold` prop that flips its button between plum and gold. `CardProps` has no such field and never will; pick one and delete the switch. Usually the repo has already picked — here, gold measures 2.24:1 on WOW's cream and §3 says nothing legible is ever painted on it.
- **A `theme === 'dark' ? {…} : {…}` map is a token that has not been declared yet.** That is the useful reading of §8's ban: it tells you *what to do* with the ternary instead of only that it is forbidden. WOW's design picks its emblem's four colours that way; three of the four turned out to be tokens already shipped, and only the blade needed a name.
- **A literal hex in a canvas is usually a token's DARK value, and the light half is the thing you would have lost (#1630).** A design tool renders one theme, so a kit writes `#60a5fa`, `#4ade80` and `#f6d76b` because it has no other way to say anything. Three of the profile-header kit's four were `--faction-default-stop-6`, `--faction-default-stop-4` and `--faction-coven-slip-gold` **as the dark cascade resolves them** — the last one under a declaration whose own comment names "twinkles" as its job. Transcribing them as hexes would have shipped a header that renders correctly on a dark page and cold on a light one, and nothing would have gone red: `local/no-raw-style-values` covers px, not colour. (§4a's colour arm, #1853, now fails the build on a transcribed hex — but it can only tell you the value is raw, never which token it was.) So the translation step is not "find a token that looks close", it is **grep the hex against the `[data-theme="dark"]` block first** — an exact match there is a token that was already yours, and a near-match is the argument for using the token anyway. The fourth of them was an *alpha over a token's own hue* (`rgba(74,222,128,.055)` = the Singularity card accent at 5.5%), which is #1169's costume: said as `color-mix(in srgb, <token> 5.5%, transparent)` it follows the cascade for free.
  - **When a whole RAMP is dark halves and only one stop has a token, derive the rest from that stop's own inter-theme factors (#2072).** The UA mandala's hue cycle arrived as nine hexes; one was `--faction-ua-glow`'s dark value to within 4/255 (the tell that the *whole* ramp was sampled off a dark card), and of the three that matched a token exactly, none was reusable — one belonged to another faction, one was the unaffiliated spectrum's orange, and one was a chip fill carrying measured ink. So eight stops had to be minted with both halves, and the question "how deep is the light half" is not eight aesthetic decisions: it is **one measurement, taken from the anchor token that already flips.** `-glow` goes `#f08a4e → #dd5a1e`, which in HSL is lightness ×0.789 and saturation ×0.902 at a held hue; applying those two factors per stop re-derives `-glow`'s own light value to within 6/255 and keeps each stop's identity — a gold stays a gold, the cream stays a pale sand, each one simply deeper. **Derive, do not invert.** Reflecting the ramp's lightness about the anchor is the tempting alternative and it repaints a cream as a brown; per-stop hand-tuning is eight numbers nobody can check. State the factor where you declare the family, so the next editor extends the ramp rather than guessing at it.
- **A kit's contrast annotations are measured against the kit's own plate, not against our card.** This has now been wrong in four consecutive epics, so measure every pairing you lift. Two corollaries that are easy to miss: the **tighter half is not always the dark one** (Singularity's light chassis is the *lighter* black, so its light half is where the inks failed while the dark half shipped as drawn), and the repo may **already have measured and rejected** the exact value the kit is offering (WOW's dark #8a5aae is the 4.10:1 value that made `--faction-wow-plum-surface` theme-invariant in the first place). When the kit and a shipped token disagree, find out whether the disagreement is already settled before you add a token.

**The level gem** is the one shape shared across every archetype: a 45°-rotated square, always outlined and never filled, with a faction-coloured numeral and a mandatory tiny "LVL" caption. It is deliberately the exception to "no uniform shapes" — the level is a game-wide fact, not a faction one, and a player should recognise it instantly whether it sits on a ransom note or a roster row. Faction identity enters as stroke colour and glow only. Unaffiliated takes the spectrum on both stroke and numeral (ADR-0039); it never degrades to grey, and it never borrows another faction's hue.

**A dispatcher's fallback is the `na` kit, so it is always the `Default*` archetype — never a faction's card.** `na` has no manifest on purpose (unaffiliated is a state, not a faction), so `pickVariant`'s fallback argument *is* its registration: whatever component sits there is what every unaffiliated player and every unregistered slug wears. Name a faction's card there and you have dressed the blank slate in someone else's costume — the #418 / #636 / #796 family, three instances now, and each one sat unfixed because the missing `Default*` looked like a bigger job than the one-line repoint. It is the same job: **the Default archetype has to exist before the fallback can be right**, and building it is the fix. `FactionSelectCard` was the third; its neutral tile is `DefaultSelectCard`, on the Default praxis card's cream-sheet language with the spectrum in three fills (the frame, the sigil's conic ring, one hairline rule). Every rainbow on it goes through `factionFill(slug, shape)`; its scalars — body ink, muted status, the CTA hairline — stay neutral, because ADR-0039 says a gradient has nowhere to live in `color:` or `border: Npx solid`.

**Badge art is the second game-wide shape**, for the same reason. `badgeArtFor(key)` (`components/badges/badgeArt.tsx`) dispatches on the badge key and nothing else — a badge means the same thing to every player, so it must not acquire a faction seam. Most glyphs are line art in `currentColor` and inherit the surrounding skin's ink; a badge whose design *is* a colour (the `duel_victor` seal carries the ADR-0039 spectrum) names its own tokens instead. Either way, nothing about a badge is chosen by who is wearing it.

Cards are arranged in a `flex-wrap` container with varied widths and slight rotations. This is intentional — they are NOT on a strict grid.

**HEIGHT IS THE ONE EXEMPTION FROM "do not regularize", and it is not a loophole (#1945, owner ruling 2026-08-17).** Within a row, every task card is the same height; this sentence used to read "varied heights" and it was wrong. Nothing about a card's height is drawn by its archetype — it is whatever its description happened to wrap to — so a row that ends ragged is not nine identities disagreeing, it is one container's `align-items` losing an argument with the copy. **Widths and rotations are the load-bearing half and they do not move ON THE DESKTOP**: every desktop `cardWidth` in the size sets and every `rotate()` stays exactly as its archetype drew it — the PHONE is a second exemption on the same axis since #2763: below 768px the mobile `cardWidth` is `"100%"` and a `max-width: 767px` block hands `.task-card-row > *` the whole line, so the no-growth sentence that follows is a DESKTOP statement (§6, under #1964) — and the class that does this (`.task-card-row` in `index.css`) is written so that the row's own items can never grow — growth starts one level in, where the flex axis is vertical, precisely so equalizing a bottom edge can never turn into equalizing a width. Read the block's comment before changing it; it names the whole chain and the four archetypes whose CTA bar depends on it. (Those four bars are inset buttons since #2030 — the chain is unchanged, because what grows is the card's own `<a>` and the sign-up was always its sibling.)

**THE SIGN-UP IS AN INSET BUTTON ON ALL NINE, AND ITS 44px IS SPACING RATHER THAN A MINIMUM (#2030).** Four skins shipped it as a full-bleed footer bar, which is the card's own bottom edge and so never had to answer where it sat; a button does, and `components/taskCard/cardCta.ts` answers it once — the tap floor for all nine, and the row with the clearance under it for the four that converted. `docs/agents/design-fidelity.md`'s carve-out is the whole reason this is written down: 44×44 is non-negotiable AND dropping it into a layout drawn for intrinsic sizes is not a port. What makes it safe here is that the button is the only control in its row, centred, with the row's padding between it and both the card edge and the card-wide link above — two unpositioned siblings, so no two hit boxes can meet at any card width. A drawn rule above the region is per-faction and stays in the faction's own file; four factions draw one, five already draw their own bottom treatment and add nothing.

The class belongs on **every** flex-wrap row of `<TaskCard>`s — the tasks board, each faction body's tasks section, a profile's proposed tasks — because the raggedness is the container's, not the page's. Praxis-card rows are a different component and still set `items-start`; they have the same shape and no ruling yet.

Each faction's archetype lives in its card component — see `frontend/src/components/taskCard/*TaskCard.tsx`. Every one carries a one-line docstring naming its archetype (metaphor, colors, headline font); that docstring is the source of truth and is edited in the same commit as any redesign. A table here would only cache — and drift from — what those components already state. Colors are CSS variables (§3).

**Singularity** is always dark in both themes — no light variant needed.

"No light variant" is **not** the same as "one value for both themes", and Singularity now shows both shapes at once. Its older families (`--faction-singularity-terminal-*`, the decode strip, the system slab) are genuinely theme-**invariant** and declare no `[data-theme="dark"]` block at all. Its v2 task card's `--faction-singularity-term-*` is a real **two-theme contract whose halves are both near-black**: the chassis stays black and what the cascade flips is the *phosphor*. Say which shape a new block is where you declare it. The failure mode runs both ways — a later editor "completing" an invariant family with a dark half, or flattening a two-theme one on the grounds that the faction is always dark anyway.

**UA Masters** (dormant, deferred to Era 2 per ADR-0004): gazette-article archetype — proper masthead, corner-snipped edges, two columns, UnifrakturCook. No component exists yet, so this line is the only place it lives — and since #1293 that is literally true: the face is not requested and there is no token for it. Building the archetype means adding both back, which is one edit and cheaper than the family costing every visitor a render-blocking download in the meantime.

**Reuse pattern:** The faction card aesthetic should be used as a wrapper for any faction-branded context: profile headers, praxis bylines, proposal form wrappers, podium cards. The card component handles the visual treatment; the parent provides the content.

### The card contract had paint but no SHAPE, and a shape written in prose is unreadable (#2361)

`--faction-{key}-card-*` covered every colour a sheet needs and its display face. It did not cover the frame, and the per-faction frame radius spent a long time as English in `praxisCard/desktop/shared.tsx`'s `frameBase` comment — *"Snide 0 · Everymen 2 · UA 7 · Singularity/Ephemerists 8 · chronicle 9 · Default/Albescent 10 · Coven's sticker 16"*. That is a real design decision, correctly reasoned (an evidence slab is not a sticker), and **a component cannot read it.** Any *shared* surface that wanted a faction's shape therefore had two options, restate the list or flatten it, and flattening it is exactly what the comment exists to forbid. So the list is `--faction-{key}-card-radius` now, transcribed rather than re-decided, with `-card-border` beside it — the frame's colour, which was already a token eight times over in eight private namespaces (`--everymen-frame`, `--faction-coven-slip-border`, `--faction-ua-card-frame`, …) and had no single name to ask for. **Eight keys cover nine viewer factions**, because `albescent` and `na` both resolve to `default`; the comment says so itself.

Three things generalise. **Minting the token does not license repainting its old consumers.** The praxis cards were deliberately left setting their own literals, because moving them is only free if it is provably a no-op and it is not: `EphemeristsPraxisCard` draws *no* radius — its own docblock lists that as a considered deviation, "the design's `border-radius` is 0 here, against the 8 the codex folio used" — so a repoint would repaint it. The divergence gets recorded, not silently resolved by whichever side the token happened to take. **A guard that reads the prose is what keeps the two honest**: `factionCardFrame.test.ts` parses `frameBase`'s comment and compares it to the tokens, so moving one without the other fails, which is the review conversation you actually wanted. And **weight is chrome, not shape** — the cards' 1 / 1.5 / 2px borders were *not* minted, because the thing a shared surface needs from a faction is its corner, and everything past that belongs to the archetype.

### A RING around a faction card owes it both the corner and the ink, and it may not be the card's parent (#2729)

The metatask picker wrapped each row in a div carrying a hardcoded `borderRadius: 12` and painted the selection outline on that wrapper, 2px outside a Singularity seal whose own corner was a literal 4. **A 14px arc drawn 2px outside a 4px corner passes *inside* the card**, so "the ring is round on a square card" and "the card's corners break through the ring" were one defect and not two. The corner was stated three times — the skin's literal, the wrapper's literal, and `--faction-{key}-card-radius`, which exists for exactly this and which neither of them read. **Exporting the skin's number for the ringing surface to import was considered and rejected: two tables is how this broke.**

**Both halves read the token, and the owner's ruling is that the ink follows the shape** — the ring is the selected card's own `-card-accent`, not the neutral one, because "the selector should change based on the card" and a shape that matches while the colour does not is half an answer. This is the exception the section above anticipates: minting a token does not license repainting its old consumers, *unless a second surface has to agree with them*. `EphemeristsPraxisCard` keeps its recorded 0 because nothing rings it; the nine seals gave up their literals because something does, and the nine corners that moved (Singularity 4→8, Snide 2→0, Coven 14→16, …) are the drift being repaired rather than collateral. Note also that a neutral surface's chrome does not extend to the rows inside it: the picker's sheet is still na's, because the choice spans every issuing faction, and only the row wears the row's faction.

**A parent's ring is painted UNDER its children, so a ring belongs on an element the card cannot paint over.** Matching the radius fixes the corner arc — the ring becomes the card's own outline offset outward, which is outside the card's material everywhere by construction — and it does not fix a card that draws *past* its own border box. Everymen's seal wears a 3px/4px paper-and-ink double shadow, Ephemerists' wears a 1px brass outline at the identical `outlineOffset: 2`, and Snide's and Coven's are rotated a degree off axis, so their corners leave the wrapper's box entirely. The ring therefore rides the row's own transparent hit target — already `inset: 0` at `zIndex: 3` over exactly the card's box — where nothing in the row can occlude it and the rotated skins cross *under* it. **Emit its `outline` only when selected**, never `outline-style: none` in an inline style, or every unselected row loses the browser's focus ring to a declaration that outranks the UA sheet.

**`radius` is one of the nine roles, so the spelling depends on whether the file declares a map.** A skin that spreads `factionRoleVars` reads `var(--sg-seal-radius)` — bare for an identified faction, with the token behind it for `na` — and a surface that declares no map, like the picker's row control, names `factionCssVar(slug, 'card-radius')` directly. The two spellings resolve to one token, which is what lets a guard follow both halves to a token *name* and compare them, rather than comparing pixels it cannot measure.

**AND A FACTION ACCENT CANNOT BE THE WHOLE OF A STATE INDICATOR, because two of the nine are invisible on the surface that has to carry it.** Measured against the picker's own stock (`--faction-default-card-bg`, `#fffdf9` light / `#1c1b24` dark) the ring reads **1.19:1 on Snide's toxic acid and 1.72:1 on the Singularity's phosphor** in light — against 1.4.11's 3:1 for a non-text state indicator, with Coven's 3.10:1 only just clearing and the other six at 5.75–6.83:1. The ring is the only thing telling a player which metatask is chosen, so on those two skins the answer was "none of them", and the ruling is **additive: keep the per-faction ring exactly as it is and put a second, hue-free tell beside it** (`.metatask-pick__tick`). *A heavier ring is not that tell* — contrast is a ratio between two colours, so widening the stroke does not move it and 1.4.11 grants no width exemption; a 4px acid ring at 1.19:1 is exactly as invisible as a 2px one. What survives a bad ratio is a mark that is THERE or NOT THERE, and this repo already had one glyph for that (`OptionPicker`'s check dingbat), so nothing new was drawn.

**A mark that lands on nine different grounds carries its own, and the pairing is what proves it rather than a per-faction check.** The badge is the neutral sheet's own pair — `-card-accent` filled, `-card-bg` for the glyph — plus a 2px rim of that same `-card-bg`. Fill and rim are 18.22:1 apart in light and 13.75:1 in dark, and for a pair `C` apart the *worse* of (fill vs ground, rim vs ground) is minimised only where the two are equal, at `√C`: **4.27:1 light and 3.71:1 dark against any ground whatsoever.** So one of the two edges always clears 3:1 — the dark fill reads on Everymen's paper, the pale rim on the Singularity's black chassis — with no branch on which card is underneath, which is the general recipe for putting a fixed mark on faction stock. It rides the *control*, for the reason the ring does, and takes the top-right corner **all nine skins already reserve for their peel `×`** and which the picker never fills, landing in the masthead's deliberately empty right gutter. Fixed size, not a proportion of the card: a tell has to read the same on the shortest seal as on the tallest. It is `aria-hidden` — `aria-pressed` is what a screen reader is told, and the footer names the pending choice in words; the tell is for the sighted player the ring failed.

### A shared surface takes the viewer's faction as a SEAM, not as a branch (#2361)

The desktop rail — on screen on every page — was hardcoded to the `na` kit for every viewer, down to a comment asserting the thing it got wrong ("the rainbow ring and the whole rail already say unaffiliated"). Dressing it in the viewer's faction is one component repointed, not eight archetypes (owner's ruling, "for now, but I reserve the right to go for B after a visual check"), and the mechanism is worth copying: **eight custom properties declared once on the container, and every style below reading one of them through a fallback** — `var(--rail-ink, var(--color-text-primary))`.

What that shape buys is not tidiness, it is the acceptance criterion. An unaffiliated viewer must be pixel-identical to what shipped, and here they are so **by construction**: for `na`, for `albescent`, for null and for a slug the server invents tomorrow, the function returns `{}`, nothing is declared, and every fallback is the value that was already there. There is no second render path to keep in step and nothing to measure twice. Two corollaries. **The fallback is per-SITE, which is how three neutral ink tiers survive a faction family that has two** — `--rail-quiet` unset reads `--color-text-secondary` at a heading and `--color-text-tertiary` at a timestamp, and set, both take `card-muted`. And **the ground moves with the ink or neither moves**: the app's `--color-bg-surface-alt` is a neutral chosen against the app's page, so a themed surface that keeps it while repainting its sheet is measuring the wrong pair — the recessed fill becomes the sheet's own ink at a low alpha, which lands light on light stock and lifted on dark, and is measured as a *veil* because a wash made of the ink can only tighten the ink's own reading.

**The one thing the seam could not carry is the display FACE**, and the reason is worth knowing before the next surface tries: `.font-display` is a Tailwind utility with the stack written into it, so there is no fallback slot to hang the `na` value on. The switch is an attribute instead (`[data-rail-face] .font-display` in `index.css`), set only for a viewer whose faction has a `-card-font`, so the unaffiliated case is the rule not matching at all rather than the rule restating a stack.

### A faction supplies a MAP, not values — nine roles and no new declarations (#2659)

The seam above was written once, for one surface, and it turned out to be the general mechanism. `utils/factionRoles.ts` is that seam with the rail's name taken off it: **nine roles — `paper` · `ink` · `quiet` · `line` · `accent` · `fill` · `onFill` · `radius` · `face` — each pointing at a token `index.css` already declares.** A surface spreads `factionRoleVars(slug, 'rail')` onto its root and reads `var(--rail-ink, <today's token>)` below; the rail is now the resolver's first caller rather than its only implementation.

**The count is the design, and it is enforced rather than requested.** The alternative shape was built and declined: a ~50-slot custom-property interface, one slot per site (`-name-style`, `-label-case`, `-edited-track`), which is a component re-expressed as CSS. A ceiling written in prose is a ceiling nobody hits; `factionRoles.test.ts` asserts the vocabulary stays under a dozen, so raising it is a design conversation instead of a merge fix.
